But every event's data reaches every player connection on the controller's stream and MQTT, so a player who reads the raw stream could see another team's code. **For review:** strip `code` from `hacking.solved` on player connections (`ws.py`'s routing) and in MQTT, or carry the code's place in a list as the Codes modifier does. **Done 2026-10-20 (M5-1):** `code` is marked `gm_only` on `hacking.solved` in `events.yaml`, and `reveal_code` `gm_only` in the mode's manifest; the controller strips both from every player connection, the read token and MQTT (one schema-driven rule, `controller/atalaia_controller/visibility.py`), so only the GM token, marshals and gm player tokens see them. Still for review: the code is whole on the radio (the boxes need it), in the controller's log and in the sync to the site server (staff only there, M5-2). | The plan's data; the leak needs a technical player and the code is usually typed at once into a box nearby. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M4-8 | Size and memory on a Reduced box (64 KB Lua heap). | `node.lua` is about 7.4 KB, 44.3 KB of Lua heap once loaded (before a collection), and composed with the Codes modifier 56.8 KB, just under the simulator's 56 KB bound (`test_mode_pack.py`). To get there the screen texts moved into the templates (the script sends which line, `note`: `enter`, `short`, `yours`), and the idle timeouts went (M4-4; the sequence's `*` replaces its input timeout). The Hacking Terminal and Codes both read the keypad, so a code meant for Codes would be a wrong guess on the terminal: they should not be combined. **For review:** a full garbage collection after loading in the core (the bound measures parse garbage too), or a manifest field for modifiers a mode refuses. | The check must stay green for every main mode with every modifier, and the margin is now small. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): keep it, with a warning for the V3 (Reduced tier) |
| M4-9 | The Field Box preset (P4R23) and the Android prop. | The eleventh built-in preset (`builtin_presets.c`, `shell.c`, `CMakeLists.txt`, the host box), the second entry of the picker's second page (M2-1), with `puzzle: code`, 4 digits, a 10 s lock, 10 points and Portuguese screens; its four templates are in the image. ESP-IDF builds with no warnings: the Field Box image is 0x175f70 bytes (19.7 KB more than plan phase 3's 0x1710c0, for the script and four templates; 27 % of the 2 MB OTA slot free); the modem and pod images build too. An Android prop offers it with the on-screen keypad (the role requires `keypad`), shown from the start for a role that requires it. The host box now prints the mode's screens as `SCREEN <template> <data>` lines, so the end-to-end test reads the sequence off the box's screen as a player would. | A box with a keypad can run it; the screens fit 320x240 (the host tests check every widget's bounds). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 14 (chrono registry)

Choices made while building the chrono registry on both sides (2026-10-20).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S14-1 | How do the marshals' keys, the replicas and the readings travel, and what happens to a reading that does not verify? | Three bodies of the field, not of a game (no `game`, one is an error): `POST /api/v1/sync/marshals` (public keys with `registered_at` and `replaced_at`), `POST /api/v1/sync/replicas` (the registry, as `replica.dart` writes it), `POST /api/v1/sync/chrono` (`{record, series_id, outcome, received_at, game_seed?}`), checked by `sync_format.py` with 3 valid and 20 invalid new shared vectors; also on the stream. A reading's `record` is the signed record's JSON **text** as the controller stored it, kept verbatim (`chrono_readings.record_json`, `TEXT`, not `JSONB`, which would rewrite it). The format check stops at the text's size; each record is then verified on its own, and one that fails is **kept apart** (`chrono_rejects`, once per text, with its reason and how often it came) and named in a `200` answer (`refused`), never refusing the batch: one bad record must not hold up the rest, and nothing is dropped silently. The same `reading_id` with the same record (in any text) is a duplicate, with another record a `conflict`; reading ids are unique per field. The controller marks every answered reading sent and logs the refused ones. | P4R15 asks for the record as received and for failures to be kept and reported; a 400 for the whole batch would stall the queue or drop the good readings with the bad. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-2 | Which of a marshal's keys verifies a reading ("the key valid at its time")? | The keys of its `marshal_id` at the field whose period contains the reading's `received_at`, the time the **controller** received and verified it, on the controller's clock, which also made the periods (`registered_at` to `replaced_at`; the earliest start and the last end sent are kept); each such key is tried (two controllers' clocks may overlap two periods); a key revoked on the site server verifies nothing received after the revocation. A reading kept apart because no key of its marshal was valid then (`marshal`) is checked again whenever that marshal's keys arrive, and stored if it verifies now. Not the record's own `timestamp`: it is the phone's clock. | The controller verified the reading with the key it had then; the same clock on both sides keeps the choice exact. A key replaced after a lost phone must not verify what is signed later; one revoked as stolen must not either. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-3 | The registry needs to know which player ids are a person's (own history, erasure, sharing), but ADR-P4-11's `field_player_ids` and the adoption of a phone's id come with registration (plan phase 16). | `field_player_ids` (field, `player_id`, account; unique per field) is made now in `0005_chrono` and used by the own history (`GET /api/v1/me/chrono`), access, export, erasure and sharing; the adoption flow (the phone's id and secret, the SHA-256 compared) stays plan phase 16's, which then needs no table of its own. Until then nothing links an account to a player id outside the tests. | The chrono registry's personal-data rules (ADR-P4-8's amendment) are about this link; making the table now keeps them testable and the rule "every table in personal_data.py in the same plan phase" honest. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-4 | The controller keeps one key per marshal (`marshal_keys`): a rotation would lose the old key, and the site server could not verify readings signed before it but not yet synced. | Store schema 6: `marshal_key_history` keeps a marshal's earlier keys with the time each was replaced (purged with the readings after 365 days); `sync_items` keeps what was sent of the registry (each key's and replica's hash, each reading's id), so a cycle sends only what changed and `sync-key --resend` sends it all again. The same key registered again now keeps the time it was first registered (it used to move on), so earlier readings stay in its period. Store schema numbers are taken in the order built: plan phase 16's "store schema 6" (and plan phase 9's "schema 5") become the next free number. | Keys before readings in every cycle is not enough for a controller that was offline across a rotation. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-5 | Cross-field sharing: whose consent, and how does another field ask? | A field shares only with `fields.chrono_sharing_enabled` (the field's admin, `PATCH /api/v1/fields/{field}`), and a person's records cross between two fields only with a live `chrono_sharing` consent of that person **for each of the two fields** (a new `consents.field_id`; one live consent per field; `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/me/consents/chrono-sharing/{field}`), through the account that holds a player id at both (never by matching per-field ids, P4R15). Field B's staff ask with B's player id (`GET /api/v1/fields/{B}/chrono/players/{player_id}`, `shared`); each shared reading carries the record as signed and the marshal's public key, so B can check it without trusting the server. The lookup is in the audit log with the count, not the player. The controller's pull of other fields' records (`GET /api/v1/sync/chrono`) waits for plan phase 16's roster. **For review:** the consent texts in the privacy notice templates. | "Both fields' and the account's consent" (PLAN.md 14, ADR-P4-5); per-field consent lets a person share with one club and not another. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-6 | Who sees and does what in the registry? | In a TOTP session (the roles need it): the field's admin, gm and marshal see readings (filtered by player, replica, series, marshal; paged), violations, replicas, keys and one player's history; rejects are for the admin and gm; the admin and gm record what was done about a violation; a marshal registers the key their phone made (`POST /api/v1/fields/{field}/marshal-keys`, linked to the account; the same key from the controller is claimed; a marshal id another account holds is refused; a new key ends the account's earlier current one); only the admin revokes a key and erases a player's chrono data. A per-game role does not open the field's registry. Every change is in the audit log. | Chrono records are evidence about players: the field's staff need them, players see their own (S14-3), nobody else. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-7 | Which readings make a `chrono_violations` row? | A spot-check series the controller judged `fail` (`over_limit`) or `warn` (`over_tolerance`), one row per series, at its highest-energy reading (the controller's own rule); a check-in or retest that fails is a refusal at the gate, not a violation (the controller logs no `chrono.violation` for it either). Staff set `outcome` (`warning`, `pulled`, `ban`). ADR-P4-1's `repeat_offender` and `delta_j` are not built: the controller does not send the class limit. | What the controller already decides; the site server does not judge again. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-8 | Personal data in the registry (ADR-P4-8 as amended; the rule of plan phase 12). | Access and export: the person's player ids (`player_ids.json`), per field their readings with each record as signed and the marshal's public key, replicas, violations and refused readings (`chrono_records.json`), and the keys they registered as a marshal (`marshal_keys.json`); consents now name their field. Erasure of an account: the signed records stay verbatim (signatures intact), the `field_player_ids` links go, the person's replicas lose make, model and tag, their marshal keys stay unlinked (what they signed still verifies). The purge, per field (`fields.default_retention_days`, 365 by default, the controller's `CHRONO_RETENTION_DAYS`): readings (and their violations) by the record's time, refused readings by their first arrival, replicas a retention after their last change once no reading names them, keys a retention after their period ended once no reading needs them. | ADR-P4-8: erasure is anonymisation that keeps the signatures; retention aligned with the controller. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-9 | Does the site server check a record's own `field_id`? | No: the field is the API key's; the record's `field_id` is the controller's free-text `[checkin] field_id` (the controller already refuses readings for another one) and is kept as a column. | The site server has no mapping from that text to its fields, and the vectors use two different ones. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-10 | A player's erasure at the field (the controller's `DELETE /api/v1/players/{player}/chrono`) deletes the controller's readings, but not the site server's copy. | The field's admin erases them on the site server too, with `DELETE /api/v1/fields/{field}/chrono/players/{player}` (readings, violations, refused readings, replicas; logged without the player id). **For review:** carrying erasures in the sync, so the controller's erasure reaches the site server by itself. | A site server that syncs must not keep what the field deleted; automatic propagation needs erasure records on the controller, which no plan phase has yet. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-11 | How strictly are public keys checked? | From the controller: 32 bytes in canonical standard base64 (the format); 32 bytes that are not an Ed25519 point verify nothing (no error). From a marshal's account: also a real Ed25519 point (400 otherwise), as the controller's own registration does. The database refuses anything but 32 bytes in `marshal_keys.public_key`, and no body or table has a field for a private key. | Defence in depth around P4R14. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S14-12 | `protocol/chrono-record.md` said the private key is also kept encrypted on the site server for recovery. | Corrected with a dated note: the key is made and kept on the marshal's phone only, public keys travel to the controller and the site server, a lost phone means a new key. This states what the code does; P4R14 itself stays open for the maintainer. | The brief asked for the line to say what is true; it contradicted the code and the ADR's amendment. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 5: VIP / Payload, and what only staff may read (2026-10-20)

Made while building `modes/vip_payload` (phase-4-site-server plan phase 5, decisions P4R1, P4R6, P4R25) and closing the leak of M4-7. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M5-1 | M4-7: the Hacking Terminal's revealed code reached every player connection and MQTT; what else is the GM's only, and how is it kept from players? | One rule, from the schema: an event's data fields listed in `gm_only` in `protocol/schema/events.yaml` (today `hacking.solved`'s `code`; `tools/protogen.py` checks they are the event's own fields and generates `EventDef.gm_only` for Python, and a `$comment` in the JSON Schema), and a mode's parameters marked `gm_only: true` in `mode.yaml` (`Mode.gm_only_params`: Bomb's `team_codes`, Codes' `codes`, the Hacking Terminal's `reveal_code`; a composed game merges them). The controller (`visibility.py`) gives them to staff only, the GM token and the marshal and gm player tokens of the game, and strips them everywhere else: the stream's `hello` (its events and game), `event` and `game` messages, `GET /games`, `/games/{id}`, `/games/{id}/log`, the read token's `/presets`, and every MQTT event topic (any scoreboard on the field's network may read those). A game whose mode the controller no longer knows shows players no parameters at all. `api.create_app` now always installs the role-aware route, with or without check-in. Looked at and left public: `code.used`'s `code` (a place in the list, M2), `bomb.armed`'s `reason`, `tag.scanned`'s tag id, the extraction zone of VIP / Payload (the defenders must know where to stand). Kept whole: the radio (the boxes need the codes; it is encrypted with the game key), the controller's log, and the sync to the site server (M5-2). **For review:** an Android prop that runs a mode holds its parameters, as a box does: a phone used as a prop is the field's, not a player's, and runs in kiosk mode. | A player who read the raw stream or MQTT could see another team's code or arming code; the schema already says what each field is, so a marker there keeps the rule in one place for every implementation (the site server can import it, P4R16). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5-2 | For the coordinator (no sync code changed): what reaches the site server, and can players read it there? | The sync sends events with their whole data (`hacking.solved`'s `code` included) and `meta.params` whole (Bomb's arming codes, `reveal_code`), as `protocol/sync.md` says. On the site server (`server/atalaia_server/api/games.py`) `GET /api/v1/games/{game}/events` is for the field's admin, gm or marshal or the game's gm or marshal, and a game's `params` only for the field's admin and gm, so the player, squad leader, VIP and spectator roles cannot read either: **no leak today**. **For the coordinator:** plan phase 20's history views (and any public or player-facing view of synced events or parameters) must apply the same rule, by importing `atalaia_sim.protocol.EVENTS[type].gm_only` and `atalaia_sim.mode.Mode.gm_only_params` (shared Python, P4R16), rather than a list of their own; `protocol/sync.md` could also say that `gm_only` data is staff-only on the site server. | The site server is the other chain's; the rule exists now and only needs reusing there. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5-3 | The extraction zone: two number parameters (`extract_lat`, `extract_lon`), as the plan wrote? | One text parameter, `extraction`: the centre as `latitude,longitude` in degrees (`38.722345,-9.139312`, at most 28 bytes; spaces and a `+` are fine, as map apps copy it), and `radius_m` (10-1,000, default 25). `field.lua` reads it at load and a malformed one (one number, a `;`, latitude 91, longitude -181) stops the game from starting with the reason (`extraction: latitude,longitude in degrees, ...`); `node.lua` treats it as no zone. Without it there is no extraction (checkpoints only; the payload's screen says so). Parameter names are short (`radius_m`, `vip_points` instead of `extract_radius_m`, `extract_points`) because every node's join carries all of a main mode's parameters in at most 200 bytes of JSON: with a 6-letter team name and the longest values the game's parameters are 196 bytes (a simulator test checks it). **For review:** a team name of 23 bytes with every value at its maximum would pass 200 bytes, and the join is then refused when it is made, not when the game is created; a `number` parameter type (the console, the app's setup, both mode loaders) if another mode needs one. | There is no `number` parameter type in the manifests, the console or the app; one text field is what a GM pastes from a map; the join's 200 bytes are a hard limit. The two numbers would have parsed the same way (`strtod`, correctly rounded on every platform, M3-1). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5-4 | When is the VIP extracted, and what does the extraction do? | The controller hands every GPS report of a player whose effective role is `vip` (BLE reports carry no coordinates), oldest first, to the game's `field.lua` as `position.fix` while the game runs on this controller (not on a standby, not paused): `player`, `team` (name), `role`, `lat`, `lon`, `acc`, `src`. It counts when the report is from the `vip_team`'s VIP, inside the circle (`geo.in_zone`: the edge counts, M3-2) and not less precise than the zone (`acc` at most `radius_m`). A payload's own `vip.extracted` (its team the `vip_team`) counts too, and the controller emits nothing more for it. Once per game: `vip.extracted` (46, critical; the VIP's `player` when it was the VIP), `vip_points` (default 100, 0-1,000) for the escorting team, and with `end_on_extract` (default true) `game.win(vip_team)`. A player in the zone who is not that VIP, and a later return to the zone, do nothing. **For review:** a phone's GPS can be faked (a mock location app), so a VIP or a payload could "arrive" from anywhere; the marshals and the GM's view of positions are the check for now. | The plan's rules; a fix whose error circle is bigger than the zone could put a VIP standing outside it "in" by chance. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5-5 | One `node.lua` runs both roles: how does it know whether it is a checkpoint or the payload? | It does not: the script API has no role. Each part answers its own inputs: team inputs reach only checkpoints (a payload's role does not use `team_input`, so the app shows no team buttons and a box has no GPS), and `gps.fix` reaches only a role that uses `gps`. The app sends a `gps.fix` without `lat` and `lon` as soon as the game starts, so the payload shows "À procura de GPS" at once instead of a checkpoint's screen, then a fix every 2 s (`PropSession.location`, the phone's `LocationService`; the permission is asked when the game screen opens). Once a node has had a `gps.fix` it ignores team inputs and the GM's owner override. **For review:** a `node.role()` in the script API (the core, the simulator, the Dart bridge and the firmware runner) if more modes need per-role behaviour. | No core, FFI or firmware change for one mode; the inputs already say which part a node plays. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5-6 | Is VIP / Payload a Field Box preset (P4R23 said no: the payload needs GPS)? | Yes, for its checkpoints: a box can only join a controller's game for a mode it has as a preset, and checkpoint boxes are the point of the mode, so it is the twelfth built-in preset (label "VIP", the picker's second page, third entry), with the manifest's defaults (no extraction zone). Picked on its own a box is a checkpoint that only captures, as Master and Satellites' boxes do. On a Reduced box the script takes about 27 KB of the 64 KB Lua heap when loaded, about 39 KB composed with Codes (the M4-8 bound is 56 KB). ESP-IDF builds with no warnings: the Field Box image is 0x1787f0 bytes (10.1 KB more than plan phase 4's 0x175f70, for the script and three templates; 26 % of the 2 MB OTA slot free); the modem and pod images build too; Wokwi did not run (R7). Its three templates are in the image (the payload's too: the box carries every mode's templates). This amends P4R23. | A mode a box can play its part in should be on the box; the payload role is still Android-only. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M5-7 | Checkpoints' scoring, and the texts. | `field.lua` looks at each checkpoint's holder every second (the boxes' reports, `field.owner`): a team that has held one for `tick_s` seconds in a row (default 10) scores `checkpoint_points` (default 1, 0-1,000; 0: checkpoints do not score) and counts again from zero; a change of holder starts over. Portuguese: "VIP / Carga" (players say VIP; "carga" is the payload), "Ponto de controlo", "Mantém premido", "Ponto capturado", "Carga", "À procura de GPS", "GPS fraco", "até à zona de extração", "Extração concluída", "Leva-me à zona de extração" (the payload speaks of itself; nothing genders a person); the players' feed says "A equipa {team} chegou à zona de extração", the console "A equipa red extraiu o seu VIP (p-…)" or "A carga da equipa blue chegou à zona de extração". | Counting whole seconds of hold needs no timers per capture and follows the GM's override and a box's late report alike; texts as for the other modes (M4-1). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 15 (scheduling, field pages and feeds)

Choices made while building scheduled games, the public field page and the open feeds on the site server (2026-10-20). Registration is plan phase 16.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S15-1 | Migration name and scope: PLAN.md says `0005_scheduling`, and ADR-P4-11 lists one migration for scheduling and registration. | `0006_scheduling` (0005 is the chrono registry's, S12-7's rule) with this plan phase's half only: `scheduled_games` and the field page's columns on `fields` (`public_page`, `description`, `address_text`, `federation_label`). `game_registrations`, `registration_replicas`, `controller_game_id` come with plan phase 16, the preset and scenario links with plan phase 20 (their tables do not exist yet). PLAN.md has a dated note. | Revisions are numbered in the order they are built; a column nothing writes yet would only wait for its code. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-2 | ADR-P4-1 has `scheduled_at` and `duration_estimate`; ADR-P4-11 adds `ends_at`, and the feeds need an end. | `starts_at` and `ends_at`, both required, `ends_at > starts_at` (a check constraint) and at most 14 days apart; times are sent as RFC 3339 **with an offset** (a time without one is refused, `starts_at_needs_offset`), kept in UTC, shown in `fields.timezone`. A registration window must open and close before the start, and close after it opens. | The names the JSON feed already uses; `DTEND` needs an end; a time without an offset would be read in the server's zone, not the field's. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-3 | Is `registering` a stored status (ADR-P4-1 lists it)? | No: stored statuses are `draft`, `published`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `cancelled` (a check constraint); "registering" is `published` with the window open, computed when shown (`registration_state`: none, `not_yet`, `open`, `closed`). `in_progress` and `completed` will be set from the controller's game (plan phase 16); nothing sets them yet. | ADR-P4-11 itself says it is computed from the window, not set by hand; a stored copy would go stale at the window's edges. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-4 | What can be done to a game in each status, and by whom? | The field's `admin` and `gm` (TOTP session) create, edit and read every game, drafts included; only the `admin` publishes and cancels (F13). A draft can be edited, published or deleted; a published game can be edited or cancelled with a reason (required, one line, 512 characters), but not deleted or turned back into a draft; cancelled, in-progress and completed games cannot be edited (409 `not_editable`). A game that has already ended cannot be published. Marshals have no access to the schedule API (they see the published pages). | Once published a game may be in someone's calendar, so it must change or be cancelled visibly, never vanish; F13 names who schedules and publishes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-5 | When does a game's iCalendar `SEQUENCE` go up (ADR-P4-12: "on every change to time, place or status")? | By one when a published game's start, end or own place (`location_text`) changes, when it is cancelled, and when the field's `address_text` changes for a published, not-yet-ended game with no place of its own (it shows the field's address); not on publication (no calendar has it yet), not for a draft, not for a new title or description. `DTSTAMP` and `LAST-MODIFIED` are the game's `updated_at`: with no `METHOD` property, RFC 5545 section 3.8.7.2 makes `DTSTAMP` the time the component was last revised, so an unchanged game gives the same bytes and the same ETag. | RFC 5545 section 3.8.7.4: the sequence goes up for significant revisions; title edits are not ones a calendar must re-confirm. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-6 | Which games do the feeds and the page show? | Never drafts. The feeds: every other game of the field that has not ended or ended less than 30 days ago, cancelled ones included as `STATUS:CANCELLED` (ADR-P4-12's 30 days for cancelled games, applied to played ones too). The field page: the games that have not ended, cancelled ones marked "Cancelado" with their reason on the game page; a game's own page stays reachable after it ended ("Já terminou"). | A calendar that subscribed keeps a just-played game instead of seeing it vanish the day after; the page is for what is coming. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-7 | Registration does not exist yet (plan phase 16): what do the window and the places say? | The page shows the window ("abrem a ...", "abertas até ...", "fechadas") and `max_players` as the places, with no registration button; the JSON feed's `registration.open` is `false` and `places_left` is `max_players` until plan phase 16 counts registrations (`ONLINE_REGISTRATION` in `services/scheduling.py`). | `open: true` would tell other sites and platforms that players can register when they cannot. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-8 | How are the pages rendered, given the pinned dependencies and the content security policy? | No new dependency: `string.Template` files in `web/templates/` with every value escaped by `html.escape` before it is put in (Jinja2 is not in the pinned set, and Mako is only Alembic's dependency), one style sheet served by the server (`/static/field.css`, no inline styles, no fonts or images from elsewhere), no JavaScript. The game page's schema.org JSON-LD sits in `<script type="application/ld+json">`, a data block that browsers never run, so the policy's `script-src 'self'` does not apply to it; its JSON escapes `<`, `>` and `&`. The security headers are unchanged. | Adding a template engine means hashes, review and an update stream for a few small pages; F19 asked for the JSON-LD. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-9 | Which language does a page use? | `?lang=pt` or `?lang=en`, else the best `Accept-Language` match (by q-value, then order), else pt-PT; links keep a language chosen with `?lang=`; `Content-Language` and `Vary: Accept-Language` are sent. Dates are written out by hand in the field's zone ("sábado, 8 de novembro de 2026, 09:00", "Saturday 8 November 2026, 09:00"); texts are impersonal pt-PT, "Equipas" for teams and "Número de federado" as the app already says. | No locale database in the image; the field's own language first, as the app does. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-10 | Which address do the links and iCalendar UIDs use? | A new setting, `ATALAIA_PUBLIC_URL` (an `https://` origin with no path; `http://` only for localhost), checked at start-up like every setting. Unset, the links and the UID's host follow the request's own address, which suits a local trial only. **For review:** plan phase 22's `setup.sh` should ask for it, since a calendar knows a game by its UID. | A UID must never change, and the `Host` header is the client's to choose. | open |
| S15-11 | Caching, cross-site reads and abuse. | Every page and feed has an `ETag` (the first 128 bits of the body's SHA-256) and `Last-Modified` (the latest change among the field and the games shown); a matching `If-None-Match`, or without it an `If-Modified-Since` not older than that, gets `304`. `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300` for feeds, 60 for pages, `no-store` for 404s. `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on the feeds only (no cookies are read there). The per-address limit of 100 requests a minute (S11-12) now covers `/f/` too. **For review:** calendar services (Google, Apple) fetch from shared addresses; at a real deployment's scale the limit may need its own number for the feeds. | Calendars poll; ADR-P4-12 asks for ETag and Last-Modified and for open reads by other sites. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-12 | Personal data in the schedule (the rule of plan phase 12). | `scheduled_games.created_by` (the staff account that made it) joins access (`scheduled_games` in `GET /api/v1/me/data`), the export (`scheduled_games.json`), erasure (set to NULL: the game is the field's and stays) and the purge (24 months after the game's end, the game history rule). The field's description and address are the field's, not a person's. The pages and feeds show no person at all. | ADR-P4-8 and ADR-P4-12: open feeds hold no personal data; everything else follows the same table. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-13 | The JSON feed beyond ADR-P4-12's example. | The example's members, plus `cancelled_reason` (null unless cancelled), `teams`, `location` (the game's place, else the field's address), and `field.description` and `field.address`; `federation_label` is null when the game asks for no number; `application/json`. The schema (`server/schemas/games-feed-v1.schema.json`, JSON Schema 2020-12) lists every member; the tests check the feed against it with a small checker that refuses any keyword it does not know. | A cancelled game is useless without its reason; a reader needs the place. Optional additions keep version 1 (ADR-P4-12). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-14 | iCalendar details ADR-P4-12 leaves open. | The calendar's name as RFC 7986's `NAME` and the common `X-WR-CALNAME` (the field's name; "field: game" for one game); `DESCRIPTION` is the game's description, "Cancelled / Cancelado: <reason>" when cancelled, and the game page's address; `LOCATION` only when there is a place; control characters that TEXT cannot hold are dropped. Checked against RFC 5545's own examples (sections 3.1, 3.3.5, 3.3.11, 3.8.1.5, 3.8.1.7 and the first example of section 4, copied from rfc5545.txt) and, once by hand, with the independent `icalendar` 6.1.0 parser (not part of the repository). | One feed for calendar apps that read either name; the reason belongs where a subscriber sees it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S15-15 | The field page's settings (`PATCH /api/v1/fields/{field}`). | The field's `admin`: `public_page` (off by default), `description` (4,000 characters, several lines), `address_text` (512, one line), `federation_label` (16, the controller's own limit); `null` or `""` clears a text; the audit log names a text as `set` or `cleared`, never its value. | Same shape as the existing `chrono_sharing_enabled`; the label is the one the controller's check-in shows. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 6: scenario packages and the objectives role package (2026-10-20)

Made while building `kind: scenario` and `modes/objectives` (phase-4-site-server plan phase 6, decisions P4R1, P4R7, P4R8, P4R24). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M6-1 | How exactly does a scenario compose with the other parts? | A scenario leads the id and is followed by at least one role package, optionally after **one main mode** and its modifiers (`org.club.foxhound+org.atalaia.respawn+org.atalaia.objectives`, so plan phase 7's `survive` can lean on Respawn's lives); a modifier needs a main mode, so a scenario's game without one takes none; a scenario after another part, two scenarios, a scenario alone or over a main mode only are refused. It brings no roles, no commands and no `node.lua`; its `field.lua` runs first; its parameters are its `field.lua`'s alone (`Mode.scenario_params`): no join carries them. A standby controller's join still names the whole game. The id stays within 63 bytes (the standby's join): `org.club.foxhound+org.atalaia.objectives` is 40, which leaves 23 for a main mode. The rules are written as shared vectors, `protocol/vectors/composition.yaml` (6 valid ids, 9 refused, 4 bad packages), passed by the simulator (which the controller uses) and the Dart test runner; the app's catalogue, which composes only what a node runs, refuses any id with a scenario in it. **For review:** a standby controller needs the scenario package installed too (it is an upload): plan phase 8 or 20 should carry it (with the presets, or in the standby's setup). | One rule set in three places, pinned by one vector file; a main mode under a scenario costs nothing and keeps the compiler's options open. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-2 | The brief said data keys start at 37 because 34-36 were taken. | `events.yaml` had 34 and 35 marked as reserved for `objective` and `scenario` (P4R1) and only 36 (`puzzle`) used, so this plan phase uses 34 and 35 as P4R1 says. Events 42-45 are all critical (a reveal, a completion, a failure and the start matter to every team and are sent once by the controller). Four vectors built by hand from RFC 8949, an example for the JSON Schema, draft RFC 0000 Amendment 4. | The schema is the source of truth, and P4R1 named these keys. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-3 | What does the `setup` command carry, and when is it sent? | `{name: "setup", kind, team, label, lang, hold_ms, defuse_ms, code, count, any, fuse_s}` (every field optional but `kind`; `capture`, `code`, `tags`, `bomb`, `off`, `done`, `failed`), at most 127 bytes of JSON (a box's CMD buffer; the simulator now reports a larger `field.send` as a problem, as the controller refuses it); after a reboot also what the node lost: `holder`, `have`, `armed` and `left` (an armed bomb goes on with the fuse it had left, without a second `bomb.armed`). The objectives package has **no parameters**: the screen's language and the objective's name come in the setup, so it composes with the Medic Kit, whose `lang` would clash. The scenario sends it at the start, every second to any node it has not set up yet (a box that joins late), and again after `node.rebooted`, as the Medic Kit's `heals`. | Everything per game travels as data to a script that never changes (K10); the reboot fields keep a box and the scenario in step. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-4 | What does each kind do on the node, and with which roles? | `capture`: a hold of `hold_ms` (3000) takes it, by `team` only when set; the GM's owner override works. `code`: digits and `#` (`*` clears), a wrong code only on the screen; `code.used` carries the team (`team`, else the last team to press its input) and never the code. `tags`: players' own signed tags, each once, until a team has `count`; with `any: true` any card counts (the design specification's "recover 3 intel tags"), for `team` or the last team to press; `tag.scanned` (`tag` the uid, `owner` the player) per tag, and the scenario counts each uid once (a rebooted node forgets). `bomb`: `team` (the attackers) holds `hold_ms` to arm, any other team `defuse_ms` to defuse, `fuse_s` (60) to explode; `bomb.*` as Bomb's. Roles: `objective` `{ requires: [team_input], optional: [keypad, nfc, display, audio, team_display] }` as planned, `bomb_site` `{ requires: [team_input], optional: [display, audio] }` (the plan's keypad and LED had nothing to do there yet). Portuguese: "Objetivos", "À espera do cenário", "Objetivo inativo", "CUMPRIDO", "FALHADO", "Mantém premido", "Código aceite", "Cada pessoa lê a sua etiqueta", "Lê os cartões que encontraste", "Local da bomba", "Armada por" (the bomb), nothing that genders a person. | The standard events the console, the scoreboard and the site server already read; the scenario decides and scores, so no box ever needs a scenario's rules. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-5 | P4R24 (who sees which objective) is plan phase 8's routing, but this plan phase is the first to emit the events. | Reuse M5-1's mechanism: `objective` is `gm_only` on events 42-44 in `events.yaml`, so players, the read token and MQTT learn that a team completed, failed or was shown an objective, not which one, and staff see everything; `scenario.started` is public. A scenario keeps secrets out of `state.field` (which players read; the example keeps nothing there) and a code objective's digits travel only in its `setup` CMD, which is never on the stream. Plan phase 8 replaces the mark with the per-team and per-squad routing of P4R24 (in `visibility.py`, with the assignments the compiler writes). | No leak from the first commit that emits the events, and no second mechanism. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-6 | M5-5 asked for a `node.role()` if more modes need per-role behaviour. Does the objectives package? | No: one `node.lua` serves both roles because the setup's `kind` says what a node does; the roles only differ in the capabilities the GM's assignment and the app need. No core, FFI or firmware change. | The scenario already tells each node what it is. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-7 | How does a hand-written scenario find its nodes before the console assigns names (P4R8, plan phase 8)? | By the names `field.nodes()` gives: the test's `nodes:` names in the simulator, a box's own name (its HELLO, the box's name setting) on the controller, `node-<id>` until a box says hello. The example maps `alpha`, `bravo`, `charlie` and `site`. Plan phase 8 makes the names the GM assigns in the Join tab the ones `field.nodes()` returns. | It works today on real boxes, and the compiler's output will not change when the names come from the console. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-8 | The Field Box preset, and the Reduced box. | The thirteenth built-in preset, "Objectives" (`{}`: no parameters), the fourth entry of the picker's second page, so a box can join any scenario's game; picked on its own it waits for a scenario. Its five templates are in the image. The script is 7.8 KB and takes about 40 KB of a Reduced box's 64 KB Lua heap once loaded (the M4-8 bound is 56 KB; it never composes with a modifier). ESP-IDF builds with no warnings: the Field Box image is 0x17dfe0 bytes (22 KB more than plan phase 5's 0x1787f0, for the script and five templates; 25 % of the 2 MB OTA slot free); the modem and pod images build too; Wokwi did not run (R7). | A box joins only modes it has as presets (M5-6). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M6-9 | Test tooling for reboots and scenarios. | A `reboot` step (the simulator's `Simulation.reboot` and the Dart runner's): the node's script starts again with no state, gets `game.start`, the game's time and `node.rebooted`, and reports `node.rebooted` (reason `power`), as `node_runner_resume` and `field_link_send_rebooted` do on a box; a test key `scenario:` (a folder relative to the test file) leads the game. The example scenario is a fixture of the objectives package (`modes/objectives/tests/fixtures/foxhound`), which neither the mode test runner, the app's generator nor the firmware picks up as a mode. | The plan's "a reboot and the setup sent again" needed a reboot the tests could make; the same YAML runs in Python and Dart. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 16 (registration and game-day sync)

Choices made while building registration on the site server, the game-day sync and the controller's expected check-ins (2026-10-20). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S16-1 | Migration and names against PLAN.md and ADR-P4-11. | `0007_registration` (0006 is the schedule's, S12-7's rule) with ADR-P4-1's `game_registrations`, ADR-P4-11's `registration_replicas` and a new `player_id_claims` (S16-4); `field_player_ids` stays plan phase 14's (S14-3). ADR-P4-11's `controller_game_id` is `scheduled_games.controller_game`, a `games.id` (UUID, `SET NULL`), since `games.game_id` is the radio's 16-bit id (S13-6's rule); `scheduled_games.walkins` holds the count the controller reports. Registrations: unique per (scheduled game, player id) and per (scheduled game, account); `user_id` nullable (erasure, plan phase 17's imports); `source` `site` and `external_ref` (null) for plan phase 17's upsert key. | Revisions are numbered in the order built; two things called `game_id` would invite a wrong join. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-2 | The controller's store (S14-4: the next free schema is 7). | Schema 7: `schedule_games` (the last schedule pulled, replaced whole), `schedule_links` (game id and seed to the scheduled game, the roster's last pull and error), and on `checkins` `registration_id`, `ticket_sha256` (hex) and `from_roster`, added in place to a schema 6 file; a check-in `status` of `expected`. `sync_items` gains the kind `checkins` (the outcomes' hash per linked game). | One schema step for one plan phase; the games table and the engine stay untouched (the modes chain changes them). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-3 | Wire details the ADR leaves open. | `GET /api/v1/sync/schedule?days=14` (1-60; `published` and `in_progress` games not ended and starting within the days, at most 200, times as seconds since 1970 as the rest of the sync), `GET /api/v1/sync/roster?scheduled_game_id=` (not ADR-P4-3's `?game_id=`: the controller's game has no site server id; `404 scheduled_game_not_found` for none or another field's), `GET`/`POST /api/v1/sync/claims` (S16-4), `POST /api/v1/sync/checkins` (a body of the controller's game, with `scheduled_game_id`, `walkins` and at most 5,000 registrations), and `meta.scheduled_game_id` on `POST /api/v1/sync/games`, answered with `scheduled: true|false`. The answers the controller pulls are checked on both sides by `sync_format.ANSWERS` (the controller before it uses them, the site server's tests on its own answers), with shared vectors: 4 valid and 15 invalid new bodies, 4 valid and 14 invalid answers. An older site server's `404`/`405` on these endpoints is skipped, not a failure. | Strict, shared checks both ways, as S13-1; a GET answer is input to the controller too. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-4 | ADR-P4-11 says the site server adopts a phone's player id "with its secret (the SHA-256 compared, as the controller does)", but the site server never has the controller's hash (protocol/sync.md's never-sent list). How is the claim checked? | **The controller decides.** The account sends the phone's id and secret once (`POST /api/v1/me/player-ids`); the site server keeps only the secret's SHA-256 in a pending claim (the secret is not kept). The controller pulls the field's pending claims, compares each hash with the one it kept at the phone's first check-in (constant time) and answers `confirmed`, `refused` (another secret, or an id made for a hand check-in, which has none) or `unknown` (never seen: the claim waits, a standby or another day may know it). Only a confirmed claim becomes a `field_player_ids` link, and only while the id is nobody else's and the account has no id there. An account that already has a site-made id at the field cannot adopt another (409 `player_id_exists`); claims go 30 days after they were made. **For review:** merging a site-made id with a phone's id later (its registrations and chrono records) if fields ask. | Player ids appear in events and chrono records, so adopting by id alone would hand anyone a person's chrono history; the hash stays where it was, and sending the claim's hash to the controller reveals nothing (a SHA-256 of 32 random bytes). | open |
| S16-5 | Registration rules beyond ADR-P4-11. | Only a published game whose window is open (a game with **no window takes no online registration**: `registration_state` is none); a display name (1-32 characters, the **username by default**: there is no callsign on the profile), a team preference among the game's teams, the federation number required, optional or dropped as the game says; one registration per account and game (a cancelled one is made live again, with a new ticket and the same player id); `max_players` counts registered and checked-in ones, the scheduled game's row locked so two registrations cannot take the last place. The player changes or cancels until the window closes; the field's `admin` and `gm` change or cancel until the game starts; a new ticket (`POST /api/v1/me/registrations/{id}/ticket`) until the start. The audit log has `registration_create`, `_update` (the names of what changed), `_cancel`, `_ticket`, `player_id_claim`, never a name or a number. | ADR-P4-11's rules, with the gaps closed the smallest way. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-6 | Where do the replicas' classes and `max_replicas` come from on the site server? | Two new field settings, `limit_template` (`portugal` by default, one of the controller's own `app/lib/chrono/limit_templates/`, now copied into the server's image) and `max_replicas` (5, 1-20), set by the field's admin with `PATCH /api/v1/fields/{field}` to the same values as the controller's `[checkin]` settings. A registry replica brings its own type, class and make. **For review:** the controller pushing its check-in settings (limit template, `max_replicas`, federation label) in the sync, so they cannot differ. | The site server must check a declaration against the field's limits before the controller sees it; nothing syncs those settings yet. | open |
| S16-7 | Personal data in the new tables (the rule of plan phase 12). | Access and export: the account's registrations with their replicas, states and outcomes (`registrations.json`, never the ticket's hash) and its claims (`player_id_claims.json`). Erasure: a registration for a game still to come is cancelled; every one loses the account, the name, the team preference, the federation number and the ticket's hash, its replicas their make and model; the outcome and the declarations' states stay as the field's history; claims are deleted (the `field_player_ids` links go as before). Purge: a registration's federation number 30 days after its game (ADR-P4-11), claims 30 days after they were made, registrations with their scheduled game (24 months). The roster carries what the check-in holds only; the outcomes carry no name. | ADR-P4-8 and ADR-P4-11's personal data paragraph. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-8 | Arrivals on the controller. | An expected check-in arrives (1) through the app with `registration_id` and `ticket` together: the ticket's SHA-256 must be the roster's (403) and the check-in's `player_id` the registration's (409); a player id the roster brought that this controller never saw needs the ticket (403), so nobody can take a site-made id first; the roster fills in a federation number or team preference the app left out; (2) by a marshal's scan of the printed ticket (`POST /api/v1/games/{id}/checkins/ticket`, staff): checked in at once, whatever `confirm` says, since staff saw the player; scanning again changes nothing; (3) ticked by name (`PATCH ... status: "checked_in"`). Expected check-ins do not count toward `[checkin] max_players` until they arrive; `pending_declarations` at the start counts only players at the field. Without a roster the app's registration id and ticket hash are kept with the walk-in. | ADR-P4-11's three ways in; the ticket proves a registration offline, the phone's secret proves the identity from then on. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-9 | "The pages show the register button only when open": registration needs an account, and the pages have no login and no JavaScript (S15-8). | The game page shows *Fazer a inscrição* / *Register* only while the window is open and places are left (also the JSON feed's `registration.open`); it leads to `/f/<slug>/games/<id>/register`, a page that says how: an account on the site, then the game in the Atalaia app, the ticket kept by the app or printed, and what data the field gets and for how long. No web form. **For review:** the app's screens are not built yet (plan phase 16's app part, S16-13), so the page names a path that is not usable yet; a server-rendered login and registration form (cookies, CSRF) is the alternative. | A login form on pages built to have none would be a security change of its own; the app is where the ticket is kept. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) for now: registration only in the app; later also on the web pages, with login through Google and similar providers (to plan) |
| S16-10 | The pages and feeds answered 405 to `HEAD` (S15 report). | Every public route (`/f/...`, the feeds, `/static/field.css`) takes `GET` and `HEAD`; `HEAD` gets the GET's status and headers (`ETag`, `Last-Modified`, `Cache-Control`, `Content-Length` of the GET's body, CORS on the feeds) and no body; a conditional `HEAD` gets 304 like a GET. The places left now change the pages and feeds, so their `Last-Modified` takes the latest registration change too. | RFC 9110 section 9.3.2; calendar clients poll with `HEAD` and conditional requests. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-11 | When do outcomes go back, and what is an outcome? | Once a linked game has **ended** (not in batches during it), and again only when they change (their hash in `sync_items`). `checked_in` for a registration whose player is or was at the field (`pending`, `checked_in`, `left`, or removed after checking in), `no_show` otherwise (an expected one, or removed before arriving); each with its declarations' final states and deciding readings (at most 20). Walk-ins: a count only. A cancelled registration that came anyway is recorded; a cancelled no-show is not. The site server applies a roster registration by id and player id, one known only from the app by its ticket's hash, and ignores (counts) anything else. | ADR-P4-11's push "after the game"; one push per game is enough for history and keeps the link cheap. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-12 | How does a scheduled game follow its controller game? | `meta.scheduled_game_id` links them (the last game made from it wins: the GM may make it again); the scheduled game goes `published` → `in_progress` when the game runs → `completed` when it ended, never back; another field's or an unknown id links nothing and is no error (`scheduled: false`). On the controller a game made from the schedule takes its title (64 characters), mode, teams (refused if they do not fit the radio's 23 bytes: the GM gives teams) and length unless the GM gives others; making it kicks the sync so the roster comes at once. | S15-3 left `in_progress` and `completed` to this plan phase; the controller stays the authority on its game. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-13 | What of plan phase 16 is not built here? | The app's screens (account login on the site server, the field's games, registering with replicas, keeping the ticket and sending it at check-in, the claim of a phone's id): the app is being changed by the modes chain in parallel, so only the Dart ticket codec and its vector tests are added (new files). `[sync] push_walkins` and the guest registrations it implies (ADR-P4-8's 30-day guest rule): walk-ins go as a count only. The controller's pull of other fields' chrono records (`GET /api/v1/sync/chrono`, S14-5) still waits. | Keep the two chains apart; the server and controller sides are complete and tested end to end without the app's screens. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S16-14 | A roster entry the controller's check-in would refuse. | A declaration whose class is not in the controller's limit template, beyond its `max_replicas`, or with a declaration id another player or game holds is dropped from the expected check-in and counted (`dropped_declarations`, logged without names); a team preference that is not one of the game's teams is dropped; a registration whose player id or registration id the controller holds for another is skipped (`conflicts`). The player can still declare at the field. | What the controller holds always passes its own rules (S16-6 says why the two sides can differ). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 7: scenario compiler and validator (2026-10-20)

Made while building `controller/atalaia_controller/scenarios/` (phase-4-site-server plan phase 7, ADR-P4-6 as amended, decisions P4-1, P4R7, P4R8, P4R9, P4R24, M6-1 to M6-9). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M7-1 | What does the manifest look like, now that P4R8 renamed ADR-P4-6's example? | ADR-P4-6's keys (`id`, `version`, `name`, `description`, `author`, `teams`, `squads`, `nodes`, `objectives` with `id`, `type`, `target`, `assignee`, `params`, `visibility`, `depends_on`, `time_window`, `effect`) with P4R8's names: `teams` is a list of the game's team names (default `[red, blue]`), `squads` a number (0-16; an assignee's squad is 1 to it), `nodes` maps the names the GM gives the boxes to `{ role: objective }` or `{ role: bomb_site }` (the objectives package's roles) or a player to `{ player: true, role: vip }` (the player's check-in role, for `escort`). Added: `lang` (`pt`, the default, or `en`: the boxes' screens, and each box shows the objective's `name` in that language, `i18n.pt.name` for pt); `params`, whole numbers only, that `effect.points: { param: name }` uses (Foxhound's `vault_points`); `name` and `description` per objective with `i18n.pt`. An unknown key, at any level, is an error (a typo would otherwise be silently ignored), and so is a key given twice in one map (PyYAML keeps the last one silently). JSON is accepted (it is YAML). The scenario id is at most 40 bytes (with `+org.atalaia.objectives`, a join's 63; a main mode after it needs a shorter id, which plan phase 8 checks at game creation) and may not use `org.atalaia.`. `author` is kept in `mode.yaml`; it is free text a GM may leave out (it can be personal data once the site server stores scenarios, plan phase 20). | The ADR's squad names (`alpha-1`) and team names (`alpha`) do not exist on the controller; one language on a box's screen; points a GM can tune without editing the scenario. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-2 | How is `field.lua` generated: templates per objective, or something else? And under which licence? | A data table, `local SCENARIO = { ... }` (every objective's id, type, assignee, dependencies, reveals, window, points, the box's setup), followed by one fixed scenario runtime, `scenarios/runtime.lua`, the same text for every scenario, with one block per objective type, the dependency wiring, the reveals and the setups (the hand-written Foxhound's structure, generalised). No manifest value is ever part of the code. The runtime is GPL-3.0-or-later, like the modes, because it is copied into every package; the generated packages are GPL-3.0-or-later (`license` in `mode.yaml`, an SPDX line in both files), whatever licence the GM's manifest might wish; the compiler itself is AGPL-3.0-or-later (P4R21). **For review:** whether generated packages should carry the scenario author's own licence for their data table. | One runtime tested once, by every scenario, instead of templates whose output differs per scenario; the safest generator is one that pastes no text into code (M7-3). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-3 | How are texts kept from becoming Lua code (an injection risk)? | Every manifest value reaches the Lua only through `lua.lua_value`: strings as double-quoted literals with `"`, `\`, newline, carriage return and tab escaped and every other control, format (bidi), separator, private-use or unassigned character written as decimal byte escapes (`\226\128\174`); printable characters as they are (so `Informações` stays readable); numbers finite and in Lua's integer range; keys that are names, else quoted (`["end"]`). Comments hold only the scenario id, which the validator restricts to `[a-z0-9_.-]`. Descriptions go to `mode.yaml` only (written by `yaml.safe_dump`). Names (scenario, objectives, nodes, teams) must be one line without control or format characters, so the GM console never shows a misleading name either. Tests: hostile names, node names and team names (`Pon"te]] os.exit(1) --[[ \`, quotes, `]]`) compile, load and reach the box's screen unchanged in the simulator, and twelve texts (NUL, every C0 control, bidi overrides, `]==]`, escapes) come back from the core byte for byte. | The plan's "Lua escaping of hostile texts"; a scenario may come from anyone through the site server's library (plan phase 20). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-4 | When does an objective open, and what happens around dependencies, reveals and time windows? | An objective is waiting, open, then done or failed. It opens when it has been revealed, its window has started (`start_min`) and every `depends_on` is done; until then its box shows it inactive (`off`). Objectives that are not `hidden` are revealed at the start (event 44, to the assignee's team and squad); a hidden one is revealed when it could open, or earlier by another's `effect.reveal` (then it is shown but stays locked until its dependencies are done). One still open or waiting at `end_min` fails with the reason `time`; a failed objective fails every objective that depends on it (`blocked`), recursively; an objective that was never revealed fails without an event (P4R24: nothing about a hidden objective before its reveal). Completion scores `points` (or the parameter's) for the completing team with `score.add`, reveals `effect.reveal`, opens what can open, and with `effect.win` calls `game.win(team)`. An objective whose assignee team is not one of the game's teams is off (a log line; never revealed). Failure reasons: `captured`, `defused`, `time`, `blocked`, `lives`. | ADR-P4-6 lists `reveal` and `depends_on` side by side; this is one rule for both that keeps hidden objectives silent. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-5 | ADR-P4-6's type table leaves the details open. | `capture_and_hold`: `hold_ms` (the box's hold to take it) and `duration_s` (to keep it; **optional**, 0 by default: completed at the capture, as Foxhound's bridge); a neutralisation or another team's capture restarts the count. `defend`: needs an assignee team (the defenders), who hold the box from when it opens; another team's capture fails it, and `duration_s` after it opened it is completed. `enter_code`: `code` is 1-8 digits written as text (a number would lose leading zeros, and the box takes 8). `scan_tags`: `tag_count` (1-100) different tags, and `any` for any card (M6-4); without an assignee the first team to reach the count. `destroy`: a `bomb_site` node, `hold_ms`, `defuse_ms`, `fuse_s`; completed when it explodes, failed (`defused`) when defused. `escort`: a player node; `destination` a circle (`lat`, `lon`, `radius_m` up to 10 km) or a polygon (3-32 corners, `geo.in_zone`), `max_acc_m` (the radius by default, 25 m for a polygon); a `position.fix` of a player with that role (and the assignee's team, if one) completes it. `survive`: needs an assignee team and `time_window.end_min`, and is **judged at the window's end**, not at the game's end: the core freezes scores once a game has ended, so a field script cannot score in `game.end`; it fails as soon as Respawn reports fewer than `minimum_lives` (`player.respawn`'s `lives_left`) or the team eliminated. Per-player lives (`per_player`) say nothing about a team's and are ignored. The validator cannot see the game's composition, so a `survive` without Respawn is completed at the window's end (nothing lost a life). | What the boxes of the objectives package can do (M6-3, M6-4) and what the core allows; the design specification's "until the end of the game" becomes "until the end of its time window". | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-6 | What does the validator report, and how? | Every problem, not the first: `Problem(path, code, message, level)`, the path a dotted path with list indexes (`objectives[2].depends_on[0]`), a short code for programs (`unknown_reference`, `cycle`, `setup_too_large`, ...) and a sentence for the GM; errors stop the compiler, warnings do not. Warnings: a destination radius under 10 m (`small_radius`), a node no objective uses (`unused_node`). Errors beyond ADR-P4-6's seven rules: a box serves one objective (`node_shared`); a target's role must fit the type (`wrong_role`); a hidden objective nothing reveals (`never_revealed`); `visibility: team` or `squad` without that assignee; a polygon whose edges cross or touch or that has no area (`polygon_not_simple`); points are whole numbers 0-100,000; the setup command the box gets, with the fields a reboot adds for its kind, fits the 127 bytes of a CMD (`setup_too_large`; a long name in the boxes' language is the usual cause); every scenario event, with a 24-byte id and the longest squad, fits a LoRa packet. Limits: 64 objectives, 64 nodes, 256 KB of manifest. Cycles are found by Kahn's topological sort and every objective on one is named with the cycle. | The console's live checks (plan phase 8) need every problem with a place to put it; codes let the vectors pin the rules without pinning the wording. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-7 | Squads are numbers (P4R8), but the schema's `squad` data key (2) is a string. | The manifest takes numbers; events carry the squad as text (`"1"`), as `team_input.*`'s squad name; the scenario events carry `squad` only when the event's team is the assignee's. | The schema stays as it is; plan phase 8's routing reads the same field. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-8 | What goes into the package's `mode.yaml` for the controller and the app? | The scenario's id, version, licence, kind, names, description, author, parameters and translations, and a `scenario:` block: `lang`, `teams`, `squads`, `nodes` (the names the GM assigns in the Join tab, plan phase 8) and every objective's id, type, name, description, translations, team, squad, visibility, target, dependencies, reveals, points, win and time window. A code and a destination are only in `field.lua`. The manifest itself is not in the package: plan phase 8's `/api/v1/scenarios` keeps it next to the package. | The player view (P4R24) and the console need the names and the routing without reading Lua; a code in `mode.yaml` would reach anyone who lists the modes. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M7-9 | The command line, the vectors, and what was left out. | `python3 -m atalaia_controller.scenarios validate` (the manifest only, no core), `check` (also compile and load the Lua in the core, as `modes.install` does) and `compile` (also write the package with `-o DIR`, or print `field.lua`); `--json`; exit 0 without errors, 1 with errors, 2 for an unreadable file, a text that is not a manifest, or no core library (`--no-core` skips the load check on purpose). Also `python3 -m atalaia_controller scenario ...`, dispatched in `__main__.py` before the controller's settings are read (a scenario needs no settings file; `main.py`'s argparse is left alone). The vectors are JSON (the console's JavaScript will read them in plan phase 8) and checked in Python only: the app does not read manifests now. `server/tests/test_shared_imports.py` does not list the package yet (this plan phase does not touch `server/`); the controller's own test imports it with FastAPI and pydantic blocked, and plan phase 20 should add it to that list. | The plan's `compile|check`, plus a validate that works where the core is not built; one place for the rules that later implementations can test against. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## First board on the bench: the on-board OLED, hold progress and game end (2026-10-20)

Made during the first hardware session with a Heltec V4.3 (two buttons on GPIO 4 and 39, no TFT, keypad or NFC). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HB-1 | The game screen is the external 2.8" TFT; the Heltec V4's own 0.96" OLED was unused. A bare board shows nothing. | Both: the TFT keeps the full screen, and the OLED mirrors every screen as a compact status (header, one big value, footer; a progress bar while a team holds). On by default (`ATALAIA_OLED_STATUS`); a box without an OLED at 0x3C logs a warning and runs without it. | The maintainer's first board has no TFT yet and needs feedback on the board; on a full box it is a status line for staff. The phase 1 ADR already planned an OLED display path. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-2 | How is the OLED drawn? | A second LVGL display (128x64, RGB565 in PSRAM, packed to 1 bit per pixel on flush), only the changed 128-byte pages sent over the shared 100 kHz I2C bus. The layout is plain C text mapping (`oled_text_for`), tested on the host, with reference images. A mode's own templates map generically (a countdown when `left`, else the screen's name; a team, owner or message below), not per template. | Same rendering engine and fonts as the TFT, no second font table; page diffs keep a countdown tick to one or two pages, so the keypad and NFC on the same bus are barely delayed. A full frame takes ~100 ms (logged as `slow redraw`), only at boot and on a screen change. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-3 | A boot splash on the OLED? | 2 s (`ATALAIA_OLED_SPLASH_MS`, 0 = none): the tower and flame of `site/favicon.svg` (drawn by `tools/oled_logo.py`, plain Python, into `main/oled_logo.c`, CC-BY-SA-4.0 like the favicon), the name and the version. Screens that arrive meanwhile wait for it. | Maintainer's request ("like Heltec does from the factory"); a placeholder until real artwork, swapped by editing the polygons. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-4 | Bomb gave no sign while a team held its button. | `standby` gets `progress` (0-1, hundredths) while the attackers hold; `countdown` gets `defuse` while the defenders hold; letting go drops the key and redraws. TFT templates: an "Arming... keep holding" text and a bar; a defuse bar under the countdown. The standby bar is now an empty track at rest (it was always full, decoration). | Found on the first board: players need to see that holding works. A hold sends a screen every 250 ms. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-5 | A game picked on the box got stuck when its timer ran out (input ignored, picker unreachable, the ended game resumed after every reboot). | The shell ends it: stops the runner, clears the saved state, draws `final_score` when the mode drew nothing in the last tick, logs `game over`, and the next key brings the picker back, as after a field game. | Found on the first board (a 10-minute game left running). Field games already did this (`box_game.c`). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-6 | Bench testing a bare board. | `sdkconfig.bench` / `python tools/docker_fw.py bench`: the Field Box image with serial keys and no setup portal. Not for a box in a game. | Without a keypad or TFT there was no way to start a game; the overlay was first a local file. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-7 | Should a mode's state survive a reboot? | **Built (2026-10-20):** for a game picked on the box, yes: `state.node` (JSON, at most 1 KB; a state that cannot travel is not kept), the scores and the seed are saved every second and given back on resume, before the script loads; `state.node { defaults }` fills only missing keys. Timers are not saved (Lua functions): the time between is not replayed (`atalaia_node_resume_at`: repeating timers keep their rhythm, one-shots that fell due fire once), and a mode makes its handler-made timers again in `node.rebooted` (Bomb: the fuse it had left, or the explosion if it ran out). | Seen on the first board: a real field loses power, and an armed bomb must not quietly reset. Replaying the time with the state restored would count it twice (the first try did: found by the reboot test). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-9 | Only stand-alone games? And does the time the box was off count? | Only games picked on the box: a field game resumes its time and starts its mode afresh, as before, since its controller is the authority and sets it up again after `node.rebooted` (changing that is a field protocol question). Game time stops while the box is off (it did already for the game timer): a bomb's fuse does not run down while the box has no power. | Keeps the field path and its tests unchanged; a box cannot know how long it was off without a clock (no RTC), and a controller-less game has no other time. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21): game time stopping during a power loss is acceptable |
| HB-10 | A power loss in the middle of the once-a-second write. | The state is written to `current.tmp` and renamed over `current.bin` (LittleFS renames atomically); `state_persist_clear` removes both. | The old `fopen("wb")` truncated the only copy first: a power cut at that moment lost the game it was meant to save. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-11 | The keypad expander bought as "PCF8574" turned out to be a PCF8574AT (0x38-0x3F) in the listing's photo. | The driver tries the configured address, then 0x20-0x27 and 0x38-0x3F, skipping the bus's other parts (0x24 NFC, 0x36 gauge, 0x3C OLED), and logs which it found; the A version needs no setting any more. | A blind probe of the NFC reader or the OLED would take it for the keypad and write to it; listings do not say which chip they ship. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| HB-8 | `expect_screen` could not say "this key is gone" (Lua omits nil). | A `null` value in `data` must be absent or null, in the simulator and the app's Dart runner. | Needed to test that letting go empties the bar; no existing test used `null`. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## The USB radio modem's OLED status (2026-10-21)

Made while giving the modem firmware a screen on the Heltec V4.3's own OLED (maintainer request, 2026-10-21), so a GM can tell a working modem from a stuck one. In software and in host reference images, not yet on the bench board. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MU-1 | How does the modem draw on its OLED: LVGL, as the Field Box's OLED view, or a small one-bit renderer of its own? | LVGL 9.2.2 with the same `lv_conf.h`, `oled_pack()` and `hal_esp_oled.c` as the box, in a display task of its own. The modem image grows from 294,816 to 483,456 bytes (LVGL, Montserrat 14 and 20, unscii 8); the 2 MB app slot stays 77% free. | One rendering path for both boards, and the modem's screens are checked by the same reference-image test as the box's. A hand-made renderer would save ~180 KB but need its own font and its own tests. **For review:** if the modem's image size matters (it has no OTA), a one-bit renderer is the way back. | open |
| MU-2 | The layout on 128x64, and where the logo goes. | A 2 s boot splash (the box's 30x60 tower, "ATALAIA", "MODEM MODE", the version), then the status screen: a 16-pixel header bar with a 9x14 tower and "MODEM MODE", and six 16-character lines in LVGL's 8-pixel unscii font (LINK, GAME/IDLE, CH, TX/fail, RX/auth, DUTY). A line that needs attention (link down, radio fault, no budget) is inverted. No periodic splash. | The small tower is generated by `tools/oled_logo.py` from `site/favicon.svg`, as the splash's (no other art); unscii is a pixel font made for one-bit screens and ships with LVGL. A periodic logo would hide the status a GM looks for. | open |
| MU-3 | When is the link to the controller up? | Up while the last whole frame from the host (any type, its CRC checked) is at most 12 s old (`ATALAIA_MODEM_LINK_TIMEOUT_S`); `waiting` before the first frame since boot, `DOWN` (inverted) after frames stopped. | The controller pings every 5 s (`ping_interval_s`) and reconnects after three missed pings (15 s): 12 s is two missed pings and some slack, so DOWN is real and shows before the controller gives up. "waiting" tells "never connected" (wrong port, controller not running) from "connection lost". | open |
| MU-4 | Which counts, and how do they fit? | The STATUS frame's own counters (`tx`, `tx_fail`, `rx`, `rx_auth_fail`, `duty_ms`), the numbers the controller's `/api/v1/controller` shows; counts above 9999 (failures above 999) shorten to 12k, 4M, 4G; the budget in seconds with a tenth below 1000 s. The channel shows the build's boot channel until the controller sets one. | The GM can compare the screen with the console; 16 columns hold both numbers of a line. | open |
| MU-5 | What does a radio fault show? | `RADIO FAULT` (inverted) and the reason in two lines, from a new `hal_esp_radio_fault()` that the SX1262 init fills at each failure ("SX1262 not found (BUSY stuck)", "SX1262 errors 0x0020 (TCXO)", "SX1262 setup failed", ...), then "no TX, no RX", in place of the channel, counts and budget. | Without a radio those lines mean nothing; the reason is the one the log gives, short enough for the screen. | open |
| MU-6 | How does the display stay out of the USB link's and the radio's way? | Only the modem task touches the modem's state; it publishes a copy five times a second under a spinlock. The display task (core 0, priority 1, below the radio's 3 and the modem's 4) reads it twice a second and redraws only when a line changed; only changed 128-byte pages go over the I2C bus, which carries nothing else on a modem. No OLED: `hal_esp_oled` logs one warning and the task ends; a failed write is logged once. | No lock is held while drawing, and the radio and USB tasks always preempt the display. | open |

## Phase 4, plan phase 17 (registration import and platform adapters) and the app's registration screens (S16-13)

Choices made while building the registration import, the platform adapters, and the app's screens that plan phase 16 left out (2026-10-20). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S17-1 | Migration and where the code lives (ADR-P4-12 names `server/src/integrations/base.py`). | `0008_integrations` with ADR-P4-12's `platform_links` and `external_game_links` (plus `external_game_links.cancelled` and `created_at`); the import needs no new column (`game_registrations.source`, `external_ref` and their unique key per scheduled game came with S16-1). The code is `atalaia_server/integrations/` (`base.py`, `testing.py`), `import_format.py` (plain Python, also in tools/check.sh), `services/registration_import.py`, `api/registration_import.py`, `api/integrations.py`. | The package path the server has used since plan phase 10; one revision per plan phase. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-2 | The file format's details and limits beyond F20. | CSV per RFC 4180 (UTF-8 only, a byte order mark allowed, CRLF or LF, header names matched without case or surrounding spaces) or a JSON array of objects (`NaN`, a name twice in one object, anything but an array refused; `external_ref` and `federation_number` may be JSON integers). At most 1 MiB (checked while the body is read) and 5,000 rows (the controller's roster bound). The whole file is refused for bad encoding, bad quoting, a column twice, no `display_name` column; a row is refused on its own with a code (`row_length` for a CSV record with another number of fields, and the same value limits as a registration on the site: 32-character names, 128-character references, 64-character makes). Other columns are named (at most 50) and their values never stored or logged. CSV replicas: `type:class[:make_model]` separated by `\|`. | Strict where a wrong guess would put wrong data in the check-in list; lenient only where exports commonly differ (integers, a BOM, LF). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-3 | Import rules ADR-P4-12 leaves open. | A published game that has not started (drafts 404, `game_cancelled`, `game_started`); the registration window does not apply (staff), `max_players` does (`game_full` per row). Rows that pass are written, refused ones are not (no all-or-nothing). An update changes only the columns the file has (an empty cell clears); replicas are compared and replaced only when they differ, so declaration ids stay. A registration the field cancelled is `skipped`, never brought back; registrations of that source missing from the file are listed (`missing`), never cancelled. The same `external_ref` twice in one file: the second is refused. Warnings: `no_external_ref`, `federation_number_dropped` (the game asks for none), `same_name` (someone with that name is already registered: perhaps on the site too). **For review:** an option to cancel the missing ones, and one to bring a cancelled one back. | Re-importing the same export must change nothing; an import must never undo what the staff did by hand. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-4 | Who imports, the source label, and the field's legal basis. | The field's `admin` or `gm` in a TOTP session (as staff changes, S16-5). `source` is 1-32 of `a-z 0-9 . _ -`, not `site`; an adapter's registrations use its id as the source. A real import needs `basis_confirmed=true` (400 `basis_not_confirmed`): the API's form of ADR-P4-12's "the import screen says so", since the site server has no staff screens.
