The audit log: `registration_import` with the source, format, counts and the names of the columns not imported; `registration_export` for each CSV export; never a value. | ADR-P4-12: the field is the data controller for what it imports and must have its own basis. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-5 | Personal data of imported registrations (the rule of plan phase 12). | They belong to no account (`user_id` null), so they are in nobody's access, export or erasure; the field answers the person's request as the data controller. They are guests (ADR-P4-8's 30-day rule): the daily purge deletes the whole registration with its replicas 30 days after its game (`imported_registrations` in the purge's counts). They have a player id but no ticket (the marshal ticks them by name), count toward `max_players`, and go in the roster like any other. | ADR-P4-12 and ADR-P4-8; a guest's outcome is not needed once the controller has pushed it. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-6 | The test adapter in a real deployment. | `TestAdapter` (`id` `test`) is an in-memory stand-in with no network, offered only when `ATALAIA_TEST_ADAPTER=on` (new setting, off by default, `on`/`off` only); a production server offers no adapter at all. No adapter for a named platform (F21, F22). | ADR-P4-12 ships "a test adapter"; a fake platform in a field's list would mislead. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-7 | When are adapter calls made, and how do they fail? | Only when the staff ask: `POST .../integrations/{adapter}/push` (publish the first time, update after, cancel once cancelled here; drafts never) by the `admin`, and `.../registrations` (through the import, preview included) by the `admin` or `gm`. Scheduling, publication, registration and the sync never call an adapter. A call has 20 seconds; an error or a time-out answers 502 with the code, kept in `last_error`; every call (`integration_call`) is in the audit log with `ok` or the code. What an adapter is given is the JSON feed's view (`PublishedGame`, no person). **For review:** automatic pushes on publish, change and cancel, in a background task with retries, once a real platform exists; the request holds the game's row lock during a call (up to 20 s). | "A failing platform never blocks scheduling, publication or check-in" is guaranteed most simply by never calling one from those paths. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-8 | The adapters' configuration and its encryption. | Fernet under a key derived from `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY` with HKDF (`platform config`, its own label, as the TOTP seeds'), the field id and adapter id sealed in with the configuration so a sealed value copied to another row does not open. An adapter declares `config_keys` and `secret_keys`; the API shows the non-secret values and `"set"` for secrets, never their value; turning a link on needs a readable configuration (409 `config_missing`). The registry refuses an adapter without a stated `basis` (its documented API or agreement), with unknown capabilities, or with an invalid id. Changing the secret key makes the configurations unreadable (`config_unreadable`): enter them again. | ADR-P4-12's "encrypted (Fernet, as TOTP secrets)"; F21's rule made checkable in code. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-9 | How does the app find a field's games? Before this, only the staff API and the public feeds (only when the field page is on) listed them, and the feeds have no field id. | `GET /api/v1/fields-by-slug/{slug}` for any logged-in account: the field (id, name, time zone, federation label, limit template, its classes, `max_replicas`) and its games that are not drafts and have not ended (at most 200), with what registering needs and the places left, no person. **For review:** this shows a field's published games to every account even when its public page is off (they are published, but not public then). | The app registers by the field's id and needs the classes and limits to build the form; the register page's link names the slug. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-10 | How does the public register page reach the app (S16-9 named a path that did not exist)? | A custom scheme, `atalaia://register?site=<origin>&field=<slug>&game=<id>`, as a button on `/f/<slug>/games/<id>/register`, plus the game page's address shown as text to paste into *Participante › Inscrições online*. The app has an intent filter for `atalaia://register` (Flutter's own deep linking off) and checks every link in Dart. No https App Links. | App Links need an `assetlinks.json` on each field's own domain, verified per deployment; a custom scheme works for every self-hosted site. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-11 | A link can name any site: what stops a password being phished? | Only `https://` sites (and `http://` for localhost, 127.0.0.1 and the emulator's 10.0.2.2); no `user:password@` in the address; the site's host is shown and confirmed before the first login to it, and remembered per origin. | Any web page can make an `atalaia://` link. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-12 | Where does the app keep the site's tokens? | The access token in memory only. The refresh token, with the username, in the app's private files like the field secrets (PD6, backups off), never the password. One refresh at a time; the new token is saved before it is used; a refused refresh forgets it and asks for the login again; being offline keeps it. A refused access token triggers one refresh and one retry. | The server rotates refresh tokens and treats a reused one as a replay that ends the session, so two racing refreshes would log the player out. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-13 | The ticket is shown once: how is it not lost? | The app saves it (at most 50) before it shows the registration as done. A registration whose ticket is not on the phone offers *new ticket* (the old one stops matching at the controller's next roster pull). | F15: the server keeps only the ticket's hash. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-14 | Sending the ticket at check-in when the site server made the player id. | The check-in form offers the kept tickets; with one picked, the app sends `registration_id` and `ticket` with the registration's player id. A phone with no id at that field takes that id with a new secret (the controller keeps its hash, S16-8); a phone that already has another id there is told to claim it first or to check in without the registration, and does not check in. | An identity that already holds chrono history is never overwritten; matches S16-8's checks. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-15 | Which of the phone's field identities belongs to which site field, for the claim? | The player picks one on the claim screen. **For review:** the sync could carry the controller's field id so the app can match them. Times in the app are shown on the phone's clock (dd/mm/yyyy hh:mm), not in the field's time zone (the app has no time-zone database). | The controller's field id and the site server's field UUID are not linked anywhere. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-16 | Making an account from the app. | Not built: the app logs in only. **For review:** the site server must publish its privacy notice (a `GET /api/v1/privacy-notice` or a page) before the app or the site can offer sign-up with valid consent; the register page tells players to "make an account on this site", and there is no web form for that either. | Consent without showing the notice is not valid consent. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S17-17 | CSV formula injection, for "any CSV export". | The site server's one CSV export (`GET .../registrations.csv`, new: the import's columns first, then `registration_id`, `player_id`, `status`, `source`; UTF-8 with a BOM, CRLF) writes a cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return with a leading `'` (OWASP's advice, the controller's `_csv_safe` rule, checked to agree); the import takes that `'` off again, so an export imports back unchanged. Values are stored as given; the escaping is done where a spreadsheet may read them. | Escaping at the export keeps the data true and every spreadsheet safe. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 9: ATAK / CoT emitter (2026-10-20)

Choices made while building the ATAK / CoT emitter, the field image's georeference and the console's map position (ADR-P4-7 as amended, P4R18). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M9-1 | What are the "known-good CoT samples" of `protocol/vectors/cot.json` (the plan allowed `cot.xml` or `cot.json`), and may we copy TAK's example files? | `cot.json`: the CoT rules the emitter must follow (the event's and point's required attributes, the type and how patterns, the time format, the detail elements' required attributes, ATAK's team names and roles, the transport), each with its quoted source: MITRE's CoT base-event schema 2.0, MITRE's CoTtypes.xml, the TAK detail schemas and examples in the ATAK-CIV repository's `takcot/`, the TAK protocol description (`protocol.txt`), the Developer's Guide to CoT and Meshtastic's `atak.proto`. Plus the values (attributes, point, contact, colour, icon) of two TAK examples, `Marker - Spot.cot` and `Marker - 2525.cot`, which the emitter rebuilds as known answers. The values only, not the files: GitHub reports the ATAK-CIV repository's licence as "Other". Fetched and checked against the sources on 2026-10-20 (one wrong attribution fixed: the `.910Z` time is from the 2525 example). | Known answers from the source, not from our code; short factual values are safe to quote, whole files under an unclear licence are not. **For review:** whether to vendor the example files under their own licence. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-2 | Which CoT types, hows and UIDs? (ADR-P4-7's table had `a-n-G-E-X` / `a-f-G-E-X` for props, `a-f-G-U-C-V` for the VIP, `b-m-p-s-p-i` for points of interest.) | Boxes and the extraction zone are coloured spot markers, `b-m-p-s-m` (as TAK's own spot-marker example), how `h-e` (a box the GM placed on the image) or `h-p` (an extraction point pasted from a map app, M5-3); players `a-f-G-U-C` with `m-g`. `a-.-G-U-C-V` is "Aviation" in MITRE's list and `b-m-p-s-p-i` a sensor point of interest, so neither is used. UIDs `atalaia.{field_id}.node.{node_id}`, `atalaia.{field_id}.game.{id}.extraction`, `atalaia.{field_id}.player.{player_id}`: stable, never a user's name (the ADR's `atalaia-{node_name}-{node_id}` would change with a box's name and carry user text). | A spot marker takes any team colour; an atom (`a-...`) is coloured by affiliation only, and a captured box is not a friendly unit to every ATAK user. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-3 | How is the image georeferenced, and where is it kept? | Two points are opposite corners of a north-up image (latitude and longitude each linear in the image; any two points that differ in x and y); 3-8 points (API only) give a least-squares affine map for a rotated image. The fit is in a local plane in metres around the points (the 6,371 km sphere of ADR-P4-9). Refused: fewer than 2 or more than 8 points, points on one line or within 5 % of the image or 1 m, a mirror image (latitude and longitude swapped), more than 20 km, across the 180th meridian, beyond 85 degrees, and 4+ points more than 5 % of the diagonal (or 5 m) apart from one flat image. Kept in the store's `settings` table as the GM's points, refitted when read: no store schema change (the plan's "schema 5" was taken by the sync). | P4R18 says two corners; a field photo is rarely north-up, hence the API's option. Errors instead of a silent wrong map. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-4 | What happens to the georeference when the field image changes? | A new or removed image deletes it (`georef_cleared` in the upload's answer, a `field_georef` message on the stream), so the feed stops sending boxes until the GM places the corners again. | The points are places on the old image. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-5 | How does ATAK show the owner's colour (the plan's test: "`__group` from the owner's colour")? | Every owned marker has `<color argb>` with the console's colour for that team (signed 32-bit ARGB, as in TAK's examples), and `<__group name role="Team Member">` when the team is named after one of ATAK's 14 team colours (red, blue, green, yellow, orange, purple, white, in English or Portuguese); black and other names get the colour only, with the team's display name in the remarks. A box nobody owns is white (`argb -1`) with no group. Players have `__group` for their team on the same rule. | `<color>` is what ATAK paints a spot marker with; `__group` lets ATAK users filter by team. The ADR's "hex colour in remarks" is superseded by `<color>`. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-6 | What do staff ATAK endpoints get with `players = true`? | Every player's latest GPS fix of the live game (staff see everyone, as the GM and marshals do in positions.py), dropped after twice the game's position interval; manual positions are not sent. Callsign: the pseudonymous player id (with "VIP" for the VIP), never the display name; the team and role in the remarks. Also the boxes and the zone. Never on UDP, whatever the settings. | P4R18 and phase 3 constraint 9: staff only, and names stay on the controller. **For review:** whether a field wants names on its staff maps. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-7 | Transport details. | TAK Protocol version 0 on both: on UDP one event per datagram (the XML declaration, a newline, the event; at most 1,400 bytes, kept by capping the callsign at 48 characters and 160 bytes escaped, the remarks at 160 and 320); on TCP one connection per endpoint and cycle, the events one after another with no length prefix (the ADR's sketch had a 4-byte prefix, which TAK's raw CoT input does not use), 2 s timeout, failures counted and logged once. Multicast TTL 1 (the ADR said 2): the field's own network. `multicast` is one `"host:port"` (the plan's separate `port` is folded in); a unicast address works too; `""` turns UDP off. At most 8 staff endpoints. The UDP socket opens on the first send and closes when the emitter stops. | What TAK's own protocol description says; a field network is one subnet. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-8 | When is a marker sent or dropped? | Everything every `interval_s` (30 s, 1-3600) with `stale` at twice the interval; a box at once on `zone.captured` / `zone.neutralized` (with the event's team, before the box's STATE confirms it), a new owner in its STATE, a move on the image; everything at once when the game or the georeference changes. At a game's end, or when the georeference goes, each marker sent is dropped by an event whose stale time is before its start (the Developer's Guide's "drop track"). Only the controller in charge sends: a standby sends nothing until promoted. | ADR-P4-7's timing; two controllers must not fight over the same UIDs. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-9 | How does the GM place the corners in the console? | Under the field image (*Campo / Field* tab), two text fields, top-left and bottom-right corner, each `latitude, longitude` as a map app copies it (checked in the page before it is sent); the four corners come back from the controller and are shown, with a numbered pin on the image per point, and the feed's state (`GET /api/v1/atak`). Read-only viewers see the corners, not the form. No dragging of corners on a map: there are no map tiles offline (E5). 3-8 points are the API's only. | The simplest flow that works offline and needs no map; pasting avoids typing errors. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M9-10 | The remote ATAK relay through the site server (ADR-P4-7's amendment). | Not built in this plan phase: the emitter is the controller's only sender. It goes with the site server's work on the controller's link (plan phase 19), and would forward props and zones only. | Plan phase 9 lists the controller's files only. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 18 (club profiles and branding sync)

Choices made while building club profiles on the site server and the branding sync to field controllers (P4R19, 2026-10-20). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S18-1 | What does `GET /api/v1/sync/field` carry, and how do images travel? | The field's branding (`field_name`, `accent`, `logo`, `wallpaper`) and its saved teams (`team_id`, `name`, `colour`, `crest`, `club`), each image as `{sha256, content_type, width, height, bytes}`; the bytes come from `GET /api/v1/sync/images/{sha256}`, only for an image the field's branding or teams use (another field's: 404). The controller fetches only images it lacks, at most 50 a cycle; it stores one only if its bytes hash to the name asked for and `images.check` accepts them with the limits of their kind **without changing them** (the site server stores images as checked); an image that fails keeps the controller's previous value and is tried again next cycle. The answer is checked on both sides by `sync_format.check_field` (2 valid, 9 invalid self-made format vectors). ADR-P4-3 item 6's "field settings, limit template" are not in it yet: no controller reads them. | Content addressing makes the fetch idempotent and lets a controller skip what it has; checking again on the controller keeps BR7's rule that only checked bytes are stored. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-2 | What does `[sync] branding = "site"` change on the controller? | Only then does the client pull (default `"local"`: nothing is pulled and nothing changes). The site wins every cycle: the field's name, accent, logo and wallpaper and one team preset per saved team are made to match the answer (`Branding.apply_site`); the site-made presets are kept in the setting `branding.site_presets` (no store schema change). The console gets 409 ("change it there") for the field's branding and those presets; presets the GM makes stay the GM's; a GM preset with a site team's name becomes that team's; a team gone from the answer loses its preset. A replaced logo or wallpaper is deleted at once, a crest after its day of grace (BR8). Switching back to `"local"` keeps what was last applied as the controller's own. The console shows no "from the site server" label yet (the 409's message says it). | P4R19: the controller's own edits win unless the field chose the site; refusing edits is clearer than silently undoing them at the next cycle. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-3 | The field's name in the branding. | A separate `fields.branding_name` (1-48 characters, the check-in poster's limit), not the field's own name (up to 128); null keeps the controller's name. Applied to the poster too (BR4: one name), with the poster's checks; one the poster refuses is logged and skipped. | A field's site name is often longer than what fits on a poster; the field decides what its controllers show. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-4 | The rights reminder at every upload. | Every image upload (club logo and crest, field logo and wallpaper, a team's own crest) needs `?rights_confirmed=true` (else 400 `rights_not_confirmed`) and its answer carries `rights_notice` in pt-PT and English ("upload only images you have the right to use: the uploader is responsible"). Who uploaded what is in the audit log (`club_image`, `field_branding_image`, `field_team_crest`), not on the image. | An API cannot show a line next to a button; making the client confirm puts the reminder in every upload path. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-5 | Who makes clubs, and with what roles? | Any account can make a club and becomes its `admin` (at most 10 clubs made and still administered per account; no limit for a server administrator). Club roles are `admin` (edits the club, its images, members and page) and `member` (listed); they need no TOTP; a server administrator counts as every club's admin. A club keeps at least one accepted admin (409 `last_admin`). Slugs: the field slug's form, unique among clubs. | A club edits a public profile, not a field or a game: TOTP would only keep small clubs out. The cap limits abuse while public pages are off by default. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-6 | A club's team at a field. | A field's `admin` or `gm` adds a saved team that is a club's (by its slug) or the field's own (a name). A club's team carries the club's `short_name` (1-32, required on every club, the controllers' display-name limit) and crest unless the team sets its own; `colour` is the colour it usually plays (a team name as games use it, no spaces). A club whose page is not public (or is hidden) can be added only by one of its members or a server administrator; anyone else gets 404. Deleting a club deletes its teams at every field. Names are unique per field (409 `team_name_taken`); at most 200 teams. | The crest follows the team: a club uploads it once and every field that saved the team gets it. Requiring membership for a private club stops a field from showing a club that did not choose to be public. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-7 | A club renamed to another saved team's name at some field. | The sync answer keeps the first team with that name (by creation) and leaves the other out, so the answer stays valid; the staff still see both in `GET .../teams` and can rename one. | The controller's presets are unique by name; refusing the club's rename because of some field's list would tie clubs to fields. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-8 | Public club pages and moderation. | `/c/<slug>/` (pt-PT and English, no JavaScript, the field pages' templates and headers) is off by default; it shows the club's colour, crest, logo, name, short name and description, never a member. A report button opens a plain form (reason: `rights`, `offensive`, `impersonation`, `other`; a text up to 1,000 characters), also `POST /api/v1/club-reports`; no address is kept, a logged-in reporter's account is; 5 reports an hour per address. A server administrator lists reports (`/api/v1/admin/club-reports`), marks them `dismissed` or `actioned`, and hides a page with a reason (`PUT /api/v1/admin/clubs/{id}/hidden`): a hidden page and its images are a 404 like a page that never existed and cannot be reported again; the club's admins see the reason. Hiding does not remove the club's teams from fields that saved them. Club images on the page use the server's one content security policy (not BR9's sandbox one) with `nosniff`. | P4R19 asks for a report button and an admin who can hide a page; a field that chose a team keeps it (the field's staff can delete it). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-9 | Club membership as personal data. | `club_members` is personal data (plan phase 12's pattern): a membership exists only once the invited person accepts (`accepted_at`); access and export add `clubs` (`clubs.json`: club, role, invited, accepted, whether the person made it) and `club_reports` (`club_reports.json`: reports made while logged in); erasure deletes the memberships and invitations and unlinks the account from clubs it made or hid and reports it made or decided (the clubs and reports stay); the purge deletes invitations not accepted after 30 days and decided reports 12 months after the decision (open ones stay until decided). A club whose last admin erases the account keeps its profile and is managed by a server administrator. The privacy notice templates list memberships and reports. | Who belongs to a club identifies people; an invitation the person must accept means nobody is listed in a club without their action. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-10 | Where the site server keeps images, and when it deletes them. | In PostgreSQL (`branding_images`, the checked bytes in `bytea`, by SHA-256), not on disk: one backup holds everything (plan phase 22). An image nothing uses (no club, field or saved team) is deleted by the daily purge a day after its upload; an image in use cannot be deleted (foreign keys). Images are not personal data (they show organisations); a photo of a person uploaded as a crest is the uploader's responsibility (S18-4) and the report button's case. | The sizes are small (at most 4 MiB, and few); one store keeps backups and restores simple. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-11 | What is left for later. | The console's "from the site server" label and a page to see the site's teams; a club's list of the fields where it plays; club logos and wallpapers on controllers (only crests travel, with teams); club pages in the app; pushing branding changes on the stream instead of the next cycle; the field's settings and limit template in `GET /api/v1/sync/field` (S18-1); a standby controller gets the branding by its own sync (it needs `[sync]` too, BR24). | Outside plan phase 18 or needing other plan phases (19's stream use, 22's deployment). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S18-12 | Shared vectors for the new answer. | 2 valid (`field`, `field_empty`) and 9 invalid answers in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`'s `answers` (an upper-case accent, a non-hex SHA-256, a too narrow wallpaper, an SVG logo, a crest over 512 KiB, a duplicate team id and name, a too long name, an unknown key), read by the controller's `test_sync_format`; the site server checks its own answers with the same function. They are format vectors made for this repository (no external source to copy from). | One format, two implementations (P4R16): the same rule as the other answers. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 plan phase 8: scenarios in the controller, console and app (2026-10-20)

Made while building the scenario endpoints, the routing of objectives, the console's scenario builder and the objectives in the app's player view (phase-4-site-server plan phase 8, ADR-P4-6 as amended, decisions P4-1, P4R7, P4R8, P4R9, P4R24, M6-1 to M6-9, M7-1 to M7-9). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M8-1 | What do the four scenario endpoints take and answer, and for which token? | All take `{"manifest": "<YAML or JSON text>"}` where they take a body. `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` (GM or read token) never fails for a bad manifest: `ok`, every problem as `{path, code, message, level}` (the validator's, `bad_manifest` for a text that is not a manifest, `lua_does_not_load` when the compiled `field.lua` does not load in the core), the id, name, nodes and objectives, the installed version of that id, and whether the core checked it. `POST /api/v1/scenarios` (GM) compiles and installs through `modes.install` (the core's load check, P4R9): 400 with the `problems` on errors, 409 when the id belongs to another kind of package or the live game runs that scenario, 201 with the summary, the warnings and `replaced`; it sends a `modes` stream message so every console reloads its catalogue. `GET /api/v1/scenarios` (GM or read token): the installed scenarios with their names, teams, squads, nodes and objectives' names, never a code. `GET /api/v1/scenarios/{id}` (GM only, `no-store`): the manifest and the generated files, since `field.lua` holds the codes and destinations. The validator's messages stay English sentences with their codes; the builder's own texts are pt and en. | The plan's four endpoints; the editor needs every problem with its place even for a text that is not a manifest, and a read token must never see a code. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-2 | Where does the controller keep a scenario's manifest, and the names the GM gives the boxes? | In the settings table, with no new schema: `scenario.manifest:<id>` (the text as the GM sent it, kept next to the package that `modes` stores, M7-8) and, per game, `scenario.nodes:<game id>` (node id to name). `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 7. A package uploaded as files (`POST /api/v1/modes`, the hand-written example) has no manifest to read back. **For review:** a `scenarios` table if the controller ever keeps scenario history, and whether plan phase 20's library should be the place for that. | Plan phase 19 works on the controller's store in parallel; two text values need no migration, and nothing is lost if the manifest is missing (the package still runs). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-3 | M6-1: a standby controller needs the scenario package too. | Handled by hand for now, and tested: a standby whose join names a scenario it does not have refuses with 409 and names the missing parts and the way to fix it (the leading controller's `GET /api/v1/scenarios/{id}` gives the manifest; `POST /api/v1/scenarios` on the standby installs the same scenario), then the same join text works. Nothing is carried automatically: a join payload (an NFC card, 63 bytes for the id) cannot hold a package, and controllers do not push packages to each other. **For review:** plan phase 20 (the site server's scenario library) or the standby's setup should carry it. | A clear refusal at join time is better than a standby that takes over a scenario game and cannot run it; the automatic path needs the site server or a controller-to-controller link that does not exist yet. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-4 | P4R24's routing: who gets which objective event? | One rule (`visibility.objective_visible`), read from the scenario package's `mode.yaml` (M7-8): staff (GM token, marshal and gm player tokens) everything; `public` everyone; `team` the assignee team's player tokens; `squad` that team's squad; `hidden`, once revealed, its assignee's squad, else team, else everyone (the runtime sends nothing before the reveal, M7-4). Anyone else, including the read token for a team objective, gets the event without `objective`, as M6-5 did, so a scoreboard still says that a team completed an objective. It applies to the stream, its first message, `GET /games/{id}/log` and the game's scenario block; MQTT (any scoreboard on the network) stays without `objective`; the radio and the sync carry the whole event. `objective` stays `gm_only` in `events.yaml`: the routing adds it back for those it is for. A hand-written package without a `scenario:` block stays staff-only. | Visibility is part of the game and enforced by the controller (P4R24); one mechanism with M5-1, no second schema mark. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-5 | How do players get the objectives' names "with the game's setup" (P4R24)? | The game (REST, the stream's `game` and first message) carries `scenario`: for staff the whole block (nodes, targets, dependencies, reveals) and `scenario_nodes` (the boxes' names); for anyone else the scenario's names and only the objectives that viewer may know, with `id`, `type`, `name`, `description`, `i18n`, `team`, `squad`, `visibility`, `points`, `win`, `time_window` (never a target, dependencies, reveals, a code or a destination: those would name hidden objectives or give answers away), a hidden one only once its reveal is in the log. When a hidden objective is revealed, the controller sends the game again, so the players it is for get its names at once. | The names are in the package (P4R8: texts never in events), and the game is what the app already follows. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-6 | How does the GM give the boxes the scenario's names (M6-7, P4R8)? | In the Join tab: for a scenario's game, a "scenario box" list of the manifest's boxes (not its player nodes, which are check-in roles); the join's `name` sets the role to that node's (another role is refused) and is kept per game (M8-2); a name belongs to one box (giving it to another box moves it). `field.nodes()` returns that name instead of the box's own HELLO name, which the hand-written example still uses when no name is given. The name is not in the join payload: boxes never learn it. | The compiler's output did not change (M6-7); the name is the controller's, so nothing changes on the radio or in the firmware. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-7 | The console's live checks: M7-9 expected the console's JavaScript to read `protocol/vectors/scenario.json`. | The builder asks the controller (`POST /api/v1/scenarios/check`, 600 ms after the last key, or the Check button) instead of a second validator in JavaScript: one implementation, which also loads the compiled Lua in the core. The vectors stay Python-checked until phase 5's visual editor needs offline checks. The editor keeps an unsent draft in the browser (`localStorage`, per browser), offers a two-objective example, lists the boxes to name in the Join tab, and opens an installed scenario's manifest (GM). A new tab, "Cenários" / "Scenarios". | A JavaScript validator would be a third implementation of the rules to keep in step; the console always talks to its controller anyway. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-8 | What does the app's player view show of a scenario? | A "Objetivos do cenário" / "Scenario objectives" section with the scenario's name and each objective the controller sent: its name in the app's language (`i18n.pt`, else English), where it stands (waiting before the game starts; in progress; completed by a team; failed, with the reason in words: taken by another team, the bomb defused, out of time, an earlier objective failed, too few lives), its assignee team and squad, and its points. An objective with no event yet counts as in progress once the game has started (the stream's first message keeps the latest 100 events, so an old reveal can be missing). The list of what happened names the objectives the player knows ("A equipa vermelha cumpriu: Tomar a ponte"). | The plan's "objectives with the scenario's translated names and their state"; words players use, no gendered forms. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| M8-9 | M7-1: "a main mode after it needs a shorter id, which plan phase 8 checks at game creation". | Game creation refuses any composed id over 63 bytes (a join's limit): the composition check already refuses it, and the controller now also checks a single package's id. The scenario's own 40-byte limit (M7-1) stays the validator's. | A game whose standby join cannot be made should fail when it is made, not on the field. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 19 (device health, firmware catalogue, remote GM)

Choices made while building device health, the firmware catalogue and the remote GM over the controller's own link (ADR-P4-3's amendment, P4R12, RESEARCH section 8; 2026-10-20). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S19-1 | The site server's `WS /api/v1/sync/stream` (S13-8) was only ever tested through Starlette's TestClient: under uvicorn in the image, with neither `websockets` nor `wsproto` installed, it could not upgrade a connection at all. What does it use? | `wsproto` added to `server/requirements.in` (pure Python on `h11`, which uvicorn already uses), pinned with its hashes in `requirements.txt` with the same `--exclude-newer 2026-10-05` (nothing else changed). The end-to-end test now runs the stream under uvicorn with a real controller. | The remote GM needs the socket in a real deployment; a pure-Python package on a dependency already there is the smallest addition. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-2 | How does the controller keep the stream open? | Its own small RFC 6455 client on the standard library (`controller/atalaia_controller/ws_client.py`): text messages only, no extensions, every client frame masked, the server's frames never masked, pings answered, 1 MiB at most a message, `wss://` with the sync's CA rules (`ws://` only to this machine, as `http://`), anything but `101` a refusal (so no redirect is followed and the key never goes elsewhere). Tested with RFC 6455's own examples (the accept key of section 1.3, the masked "Hello" of section 5.7) and a raw-socket server. | The packaged `websockets` 10.4 has no client for a plain thread, and the site server's test image (where the end-to-end test runs the controller) has no `websockets`: no new dependency on either side (S13-8's rule). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-3 | What travels on the stream for the remote GM? | Only with `[sync] remote_gm = true` does the controller open the stream at all. It sends `hello` (`remote_gm`), `live` (its live game: game, name, mode, teams, state, whether it is in charge, totals, the nodes with the health fields of `POST /sync/nodes`, the latest 50 events, the game's declared mode commands; or null), at most every 2 s when something changed and every 30 s anyway, and `command.result` (`done` or a code: `remote_gm_off`, `invalid`, `stale`, `not_live`, `not_found`, `conflict`, `unavailable`, `failed`, with a detail of up to 200 characters). The site server sends `command` (`id`, `game`, `command`, `params`, `user {id, username}`, `sent_at`). Both sides check every message with `sync_format.STREAM`; only `hello` is answered. 13 valid and 24 invalid shared vectors under `stream` in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`, format vectors made for this repository from `protocol/sync.md` (no external source). | One format, two implementations (P4R16); a socket that exists only when the field turned the feature on is the smallest attack surface. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-4 | Who may send a remote command, what, and to which controller? | A user with the **`gm` role on that field**, in a session that passed the TOTP step (ADR-P4-2: `gm` counts only then); not the field's admin, not a per-game gm, not a server administrator unless they also hold `gm` on the field (403 `role_required`). Only `start`, `pause` (optional reason), `resume`, `end` (optional reason), `message` (1-100 characters to every node) and `mode` (a declared mode command by name, optionally to one node); never score adjustments, ownership, reset, reboot, joins, check-ins or setup. A command names the game (`game_id`, `game_seed`), so it cannot act on another game than the one the user saw. It goes to the controller named (`controller_id`) or to the only connected controller with `remote_gm` on and in charge of its game (else 409 `controller_required`); 409 `controller_offline` / `remote_gm_off`, 504 `controller_timeout` after 10 s, 409 `refused_by_controller` with its code; 30 commands a minute per account. The links live in memory in the server's one worker (ADR-P4-2); a revoked API key drops its links at once. | P4R12's list and rules, read strictly: the remote path should be no wider than what a GM at the field does in a game, and never reach what changes the result (scores, ownership) or the boxes (reboot, reset). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-5 | What does the controller check before it acts? | Everything again, whatever the site server checked: the command's form with the same `sync_format.check_command`; `[sync] remote_gm` (off: every command refused, even one that arrives); the game is its live game (`not_live` otherwise); `sent_at` within 60 s of its own clock (`stale`: a delayed command never runs late, at the price of needing a set clock); a command id it has seen is answered from its log and never carried out twice; then the engine's own methods, exactly as the console's GM token (a standby refuses, a mode command must be one the game's manifests declare for the node's role). | The controller is authoritative (ADR-P4-3) and must not trust the site server more than its own console; a compromised site server can then do no more than a GM could, and only on a field that turned it on. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-6 | How is each command logged on both sides? | Site server: the audit log, `remote_gm_command` (the command's id, the controller, the game, the command, its parameters and the result) for every command sent and `remote_gm_denied` (the reason: role, form, offline, off) for every attempt refused before it left, each with the account and address; `GET /api/v1/fields/{field}/remote-gm/log` for the field's admin and gm. Controller: a new table `remote_commands` (store schema 8: the command's id, when, the site server's account id and username, the command, parameters, game and result), kept 365 days, shown at `GET /api/v1/remote-gm` (GM token) with the stream's state, and a warning line in its log for each command. No console screen yet. | "Logged on both sides" (P4R12): each side keeps its own record, so neither has to trust the other's. The username on the controller names who acted; it is a staff account, kept as long as the chrono registry. **For the coordinator:** schema 8 may collide with plan phase 8 if that also adds a store schema; renumber on rebase. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-7 | What does the remote GM see? | `GET /api/v1/fields/{field}/remote-gm` (the field's gm): each connected controller, whether it takes commands, when its live view came and the view itself, polled (no push to the browser yet). **No positions**: ADR-P4-8's amendment allows a remote GM to see them live with `remote_gm` on, but this version sends none (the live view's check refuses a position anywhere, as the sync's events do); the live view is kept in memory only, never stored. The site server has no GM web page yet: this is the API a page or the app will use. | The safer reading of P4R25 until a page that needs positions exists; what the GM needs to run a game remotely is the state, the score and the boxes' health. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-8 | How does the firmware catalogue keep a box from installing an unsigned or unverified image? (The ADR does not say; RESEARCH section 8 says "signed".) | The catalogue is a list only: no image is stored, served or sent to a controller or a box (OTA is not built). An entry (platform, version, size, SHA-256) is accepted only with an Ed25519 signature, by a release key the server is configured to trust (`ATALAIA_FIRMWARE_KEYS`, at most 8; none: the catalogue takes nothing, 409 `no_release_keys`), over a fixed manifest text (`atalaia-firmware-release-v1`, one `key=value` per line); entries are checked again at every listing, so a key removed from the setting withdraws everything it signed. Server administrators add and withdraw entries (a withdrawn one stays, marked, with its reason); every add, refusal and withdrawal is audited. A pre-release (`-rc.1`) is never "the latest"; versions order as Semantic Versioning. When OTA is built, the box must verify the image's signature itself with keys in its own firmware before installing (RESEARCH's A/B with Ed25519); the site server's check never replaces it. | The safe option: nothing installable exists until OTA does, and the list itself cannot advertise an image no release key signed. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-9 | Where does device health come from, and what is a device? | From what controllers already sync, with no table of its own: each game's nodes (battery, RSSI, SNR, platform, firmware and core versions, tier, last seen) and their `node.online`, `node.offline`, `battery.low` and `node.tamper` events (the event's node is the node it is about). Node ids are per game, so across games a device is its **name** (HELLO's `node_name`, set on the box); `GET /api/v1/fields/{field}/devices` lists each name's latest entry among the games synced in the last 90 days (1-730), `GET /api/v1/games/{game}/devices` one game's nodes; the field's admin and gm read them. Flags: battery low under 20 % (or a `battery.low` event without a level), offline when the last link event is `node.offline`, silent when a live game's node was last seen more than 180 s ago, tampered, and firmware outdated against the catalogue's latest verified stable release for its platform (HELLO's platform byte). Nothing emits `node.online`, `node.offline` or `battery.low` yet (they are in the schema only): health reads them when firmware or the controller start to. | Every number already reaches the site server through the sync; a hardware id would need a HELLO change (an RFC), so the box's own name is the best identity until then. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-10 | M9-10: the remote ATAK relay through the site server (ADR-P4-7's amendment) was deferred to this plan phase. Is it built? | **No.** The stream it would ride is built, but forwarding CoT to remote ATAK clients means a TCP endpoint on the internet; raw CoT on 4242 (what the ADR sketches) has no authentication, so anyone could read the field's markers, and TAK's own answer (TLS streaming on 8089 with client certificates) needs certificate enrolment for each device. Deferred to a later plan phase (or an RFC) that designs that enrolment; plan phase 19's text does not list it. The emitter on the controller (props and zones on the field's multicast, staff endpoints over TCP) is unchanged. | A high-risk path (P4R12) should not open an unauthenticated port to carry game data; doing it right is its own piece of work. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S19-11 | What is left for later. | A GM page on the site server (the API is built), pushing the live view to the browser instead of polling, the controller console's screen for `remote_commands`, positions in the remote view (S19-7), the remote ATAK relay (S19-10), more than one server worker (the links are in memory), OTA and serving images (S19-8), a hardware id in HELLO (S19-9), emitting `node.online`/`node.offline`/`battery.low`. A real deployment over 4G is HW1. | Outside plan phase 19, or needing other plan phases, hardware or an RFC. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 20 (game history and the scenario library)

Choices made while building the game history views and the field's scenario library on the site server, and its pull on the field controller (2026-10-20). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S20-1 | M8-3: should the site's scenario library carry a scenario to a standby controller automatically? | Yes. Every controller with the field's API key pulls the same library (`GET /api/v1/sync/presets`: each live scenario by id, revision and the SHA-256 of its manifest, and the field's game presets) with `[sync] library = true`, **on by default** once sync is on, and fetches a manifest (`GET /api/v1/sync/scenarios/{id}`, its SHA-256 checked) only when it differs from the one it last installed from the site (setting `sync.site_library`, no store schema change). It installs it with `scenario_service.install`, the console's own install (compiled, `field.lua` loaded in the core again, the manifest kept beside the package), so the primary and every standby have it and a standby's join for a scenario game works without carrying anything by hand (tested with the real controller and site server, and with the join text in the controller's tests). A refused manifest is not fetched again until the site changes it; one the live game runs waits for the game's end (the console's rule); a GM's local install of the same id stays until the site's version changes. Scenarios and presets the site no longer lists stay on the controller. The manual path of M8-3 (read the manifest on the primary, install it on the standby) still works without a site server. | The site server is the one place both controllers of a field reach; the controller stays the authority (P4R9) because it checks again; pulling only on change keeps a 4G link quiet. Default on, unlike branding (S18-2), because pulling adds and never takes away. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-2 | How is the library stored and versioned? | `field_scenarios` (one per field and package id, the current `revision`, `archived_at`) and `field_scenario_revisions` (every save: the manifest text exactly as typed, its SHA-256, the manifest's version and name, the validator's warnings); saving the same text again changes nothing (`unchanged`), a new text is the next revision, an archived scenario saved again is `restored`. The newest 50 revisions of a scenario are kept. At most 200 live scenarios and 200 presets per field (one sync answer). No author column: who saved or archived what is in the audit log (`scenario_created`, `_revised`, `_restored`, `_archived`; the id and revision, never the manifest). Archiving (`DELETE`) stops the push; there is no hard delete yet. | "Store, version" (the plan) with the least personal data; the text as typed lets the GM read back what they wrote, comments included. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-3 | The score timeline from per-node counters: where do the times come from? | Every rise of a counter the site server stores (`POST /api/v1/sync/scores`'s `GREATEST` upsert, now returning what it raised) is kept in `game_score_samples` with its arrival time; the timeline replays samples and `score.delta` events in arrival order with the controller's own rule (`sync_format.totals`), one point per arrival, its `t` in seconds from the game's start; at most 1,000 points (evenly thinned, the last kept). The resolution is the sync interval (30 s during a game), not the radio's. Games synced before this migration have no samples, so their timeline is flat until their counters next rise. | The counters merge by maximum and carry no time; the controller's own game time is per node and stops when paused, so the site server's arrival time is the one clock both counters and adjustments share. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-4 | Game presets on the site server. | `field_presets` (name unique per field, mode id, parameters as JSON up to 8 KiB), managed by the field's admin and gm, pushed whole in the presets answer. The site server checks only the form (the shared `sync_format.check_presets`): it does not know which modes a controller has. The controller saves a preset by its name once its mode is installed and `resolve_params` accepts its parameters; until then it is named in `sync.library.error` and tried again each cycle. A site preset replaces a GM preset with the same name when the site's preset changes; presets the site drops stay on the controller. | ADR-P4-1's `presets` and ADR-P4-3's "GET /sync/presets (presets and scenarios)"; a scenario game is created from a preset in practice. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-5 | Who sees which history, and what does "spectator views with a delay" hide? | Staff (the field's admin, gm and marshal, a game's gm or marshal) see the log, timeline and totals as they are. Anyone else with a role on the field or the game (player, squad leader, VIP, spectator) sees a **live** game's log, totals and timeline `spectator_delay_s` behind (per field, 0-3,600 s, **60 s by default**), and a log without the `gm_only` data (`visibility.public_event`, the controller's rule), without telemetry events and without the players' pseudonymous ids (`player`, `owner`, `holder`) and `replica`. An ended game has no delay. The field's list (`GET /api/v1/fields/{id}/history`) needs a role on the field; a game's views a role on the field or the game. The public scoreboard (`fields.public_scoreboard`, off by default, `/f/<slug>/scoreboard` and `.json`) shows the live game (or one that ended in the last 12 hours): name, mode, state, teams with display names and delayed scores, the winner; no events, players, parameters or nodes. | A delay keeps the history from becoming live intelligence for players on the field; pseudonymous ids are still personal data and spectators do not need them. A minute is short enough for a scoreboard and long enough to be useless as intel. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-6 | Personal data (ADR-P4-8's rule) and the history's retention. | Access and export gain `game_history` (`game_history.json`): the games whose events name one of the person's player ids (`field_player_ids`) as `player`, `owner` or `holder`, with those events and the game's totals, plus the games their registrations were checked in to. Erasure needs nothing new: deleting `field_player_ids` (plan phase 14) removes the only link; the events keep the field's pseudonymous ids. The new tables hold no personal data. Retention: ADR-P4-8's "game history 24 months, per field" becomes `field_history_settings.retention_days` (30-730, 730 by default): the daily purge deletes a game (with its events, counters, samples, nodes and roles) that many days after it ended, so a field may keep less than 24 months, never more. Not done: an index on the events' `player` key (the access query scans the field's events), a per-field setting for the scheduled games' 24 months. | ADR-P4-8 asks for the 24 months per field; a ceiling at the ADR's value keeps the privacy notice true for every field. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-7 | Shared code and formats. | The site server checks a manifest with `atalaia_controller.scenarios` (validate, compile) and the core (`check_package`, the core library loaded once per process, its calls serialised) in `services/library.py`; without the core a manifest can be checked but not saved (`503 core_unavailable`), as the controller refuses an install without its core. The two new answers are `sync_format.check_presets` and `check_scenario` (plain Python, P4R16) with 3 valid and 9 invalid new answer vectors in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`; `test_shared_imports` now also covers `atalaia_controller.scenarios` and `visibility`. New models are in `atalaia_server/history_models.py` (imported at the end of `models.py`); the migration is `0011_history`, chained after plan phase 19's `0010_firmware`. | One checker on both sides; the separate module and migration keep plan phase 19, built at the same time, apart. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S20-8 | What is left for later. | A console or web page for the history and the library (this plan phase is the API and the public scoreboard page); pushing the library on the stream instead of the next cycle; removing a scenario or preset from controllers when the site archives or deletes it; a field-wide history across fields (each field's history is its own); score timelines in game time rather than arrival time; the history views in the app. | Scope M: the API, the sync and the tests. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4, plan phase 21 (hosted multi-tenant mode)

Choices made while building hosted mode: one site server for many fields, each in a PostgreSQL schema of its own with row-level security under it, and the operator's commands to make, migrate, suspend, export and delete one field (ADR-P4-4 as built; 2026-10-20). In software, tested against PostgreSQL 16 in Docker, not yet on a real hosted deployment; it does not go live before the legal review (P4-6). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| S21-1 | Schema per field, row-level security, or both? ADR-P4-4 has both, but its RLS policy compares `field_id`, which many tables do not have (`users`, `sessions`, `game_events`, `branding_images`...), and the plan's brief asks that a routing bug show nothing. | Both. Schema per field (`field_<slug>`, hyphens as underscores) is the primary isolation; under it every table of a field's schema has `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and one policy, `atalaia_tenant`, for reading and writing (`USING` and `WITH CHECK`): `current_setting('atalaia.tenant', true)` = that schema's name, a constant per schema, so it covers every table, with or without `field_id`. A missing setting reads and writes nothing; another field's setting (a session pointed at the wrong schema) reads and writes nothing there. | The safe option where the ADR contradicts itself: the policy must cover every table, and a constant per schema needs no column. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-2 | What is a tenant? | One field, and everything a single-field server has: a tenant's schema holds every table at the same Alembic revision, accounts and clubs included, so a player at two hosted fields has two accounts and erasure, access and export stay per field. One field per tenant: `POST /api/v1/fields` answers `403 hosted_one_field` in hosted mode (the operator's `hosted create` makes the field). Cross-field chrono sharing does not cross tenants (ADR-P4-4's "cross-field lookup tables" in `public` are not built). | ADR-P4-4's schema per field puts `users` in each schema; a tenant that is exactly a single-field database exports to and imports from one (ADR-P4-4's migration path) and shares nothing by construction. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-3 | How is the tenant found? ADR-P4-4: by API key or by host name; its re-check: the API key also selects the tenant on the sync WebSocket. | By host name only: `<slug>.<ATALAIA_HOSTED_DOMAIN>` (port and case ignored), looked up in `public.atalaia_tenants` on every request (`tenancy.TenantMiddleware`, a pure ASGI middleware, so the WebSocket too). Any other name: `404 unknown_field` (a socket closed with 4404); a suspended field: `403 field_suspended`; database down: 503. Only `GET /api/v1/health` answers without a field (the container's health check calls 127.0.0.1). A controller's API key is looked up inside the tenant its host chose, so a key of another field is just unknown there (401, a socket closed with 4401). | One rule for people, pages and controllers; a key that could pick its own tenant would need a hub-wide key table in `public` and would let one wrong lookup cross fields. Controllers already take a server URL. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-4 | The settings, and the deployment. | `ATALAIA_HOSTED` (`on`/`off`, off by default) and `ATALAIA_HOSTED_DOMAIN` (required in hosted mode, a domain of two labels or more, no port), with `ATALAIA_PUBLIC_URL` refused in hosted mode: each field's pages and feeds link to `https://<slug>.<domain>`. Off, nothing of this runs: the full existing suite passes unchanged. `docker-compose.yml` stays the single-field deployment; DEPLOY.md's "Hosted mode" gives the procedure by hand (two roles, the settings, uvicorn without the image's `alembic upgrade`, a wildcard name and certificate). | Hosted mode is explicit, and not live before P4-6; a hosted Compose file with wildcard TLS is a deployment decision for when the hub is real. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-5 | Which database roles, and how is the per-request setting kept from leaking? | Two roles: the **owner** (owns the schemas, runs the migrations and the operator's commands) and the **server's role** (no superuser, no `BYPASSRLS`, owns nothing; `USAGE` on the field schemas, `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` on their tables, never `TRUNCATE` (which RLS does not cover), `REFERENCES` or `TRIGGER`, nothing on `alembic_version`, `SELECT` only on the registry, nothing on the hub's audit log). The server refuses to start when its role is a superuser, has `BYPASSRLS`, owns a field's table or schema, may change the registry, or finds no registry (`tenancy.check_app_role`). Every transaction of a hosted session starts with `set_config('search_path', <schema>, true)` and `set_config('atalaia.tenant', <schema>, true)` (transaction-local, the `SET LOCAL` form; SQLAlchemy's `after_begin` on `tenancy.TenantSession`, so a session that commits and goes on is set up again); the engine's connections start with `search_path=pg_catalog`, so a session with no tenant, or a plain connection, resolves no table at all. `FORCE` applies the policies to the owner too; the operator's commands set the tenant for themselves. | The brief's rules for RLS; a pooled connection is tested to carry nothing on (one connection shared by two fields' requests). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-6 | Tokens, lockouts and in-memory state across tenants. | Access tokens and the TOTP login step carry `ten` (the schema) and are refused in another tenant, and in single-field mode a token with `ten` is refused; refresh tokens are per schema already. The login lockout key and the TOTP lockout key include the tenant; the remote GM's links are keyed by (tenant, field, controller) and every lookup is in the current tenant. One `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY` for the hub (TOTP seeds and platform credentials are sealed under it). The per-address limits (100 requests a minute, registrations, reports, the address lockout) stay hub-wide. | A token or a lockout of field A must never count at field B, even if an id repeated; per-address limits protect the hub itself. Per-tenant keys would add key management for no gain the claim does not give. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-7 | How are the hub's own tables and the tenants' migrations kept? | The registry (`public.atalaia_tenants`: slug, schema, field id, status `active`/`suspended`, times) and the hub's audit log (`public.atalaia_hub_audit`) are made by `hosted init` with idempotent DDL, not by Alembic. Each tenant has its own `alembic_version` in its schema; `migrations/env.py` takes `Config.attributes["schema"]`, sets the path, the tenant setting and `version_table_schema`; `hosted migrate` runs every tenant (active or suspended) to head and applies the grants and policies again, so a new table gets them. With `ATALAIA_HOSTED=on` a plain `alembic upgrade` refuses to run. `hosted init` refuses a database whose `public` already has the single-field tables. | One migration history for both modes; nothing of a field ever lands in `public`. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-8 | Export: ADR-P4-4's `pg_dump --schema` and `GET /api/v1/admin/export`? | The operator's `hosted export SLUG --out FILE --reason`: a ZIP of `manifest.json` (format `atalaia-field-export` 1, slug, field id, revision, time, each table's columns, rows and SHA-256, in load order), `README.txt` and every table of the schema as CSV (`COPY ... TO STDOUT`), read in one repeatable-read, read-only transaction; mode 0600, never overwriting a file; audited with its SHA-256. `python -m atalaia_server import-field FILE` loads it into an empty single-field database at the same revision in one transaction (checksums, columns, the seeded `roles` compared, sequences reset), then deletes sessions and refresh tokens, resets TOTP enrolments and turns platform links off without their configuration (sealed under the hub's key). No API endpoint. | The image has no `pg_dump` and the format must be importable without one; the export holds every account's password hash and every key's hash, so it is the processor's act on the controller's instruction (the DPA's data return), not an endpoint behind one admin login. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-9 | Deletion, and what the personal-data rules ask. | `hosted delete SLUG --confirm SLUG --reason TEXT`: in one transaction the schema is dropped with every row (`DROP SCHEMA ... CASCADE`) and the registry row deleted; the hub's audit log keeps the action, slug, schema, field id, actor, reason and row counts per table, never a row or a name. `hosted suspend`/`resume` refuse or serve a field without deleting. The backups still hold a deleted field until they expire (30 days): DEPLOY.md tells the operator to say so in the written confirmation the DPA asks for. A socket a controller holds open fails at its next message. | ADR-P4-4's atomic deletion; ADR-P4-8 and the DPA: a record that the deletion happened, without keeping what was deleted. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-10 | The purge and the first administrator in hosted mode. | The daily retention purge and `python -m atalaia_server purge` run once per active field, each in its own tenant (one field's failure does not stop the others); `create-admin --field SLUG` is required in hosted mode (and refused without it) and makes that field's administrator, whose `server_admin` reaches only its own schema. | Every rule of ADR-P4-8 applies per field, as on a field's own server. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-11 | The hosted operator's limits. | The hub operator manages fields only with the owner role's commands, each audited with `--actor` and `--reason`; through the API the operator's `ATALAIA_METRICS_TOKEN` reads one field's counts at that field's address (no personal data) and nothing else (401 on every data endpoint). A field's administrator is limited to its schema: other fields' ids answer 403 or 404 (or, for a server administrator's list of a field id that is not in its schema, an empty list), never their rows. | The DPA: the processor acts only on documented instructions; the field is the controller. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-12 | What is left for later. | A hosted Compose file with wildcard or on-demand TLS; custom domains per field; restoring a hosted hub's backup (owners and roles kept; `restore.sh` is single-field); cross-field chrono sharing between tenants; per-field quotas or plans; an export a field's administrator can download; closing a deleted field's open sockets at once; ADR-P4-4's ~1,000 schemas per PostgreSQL limit (a second instance beyond that); a real hosted deployment and the legal review (P4-6). | Scope M; nothing of these weakens the isolation. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S21-13 | Coordinator's review note (2026-10-21): what the row-level security does not stop. | Nothing changed: the policy keys on a session setting the server's own role may set (`set_config('atalaia.tenant', ...)`), as every setting-based RLS design does, so a SQL injection in the app could switch fields; the defence there is that all the app's SQL is parameterised (SQLAlchemy) and the schemas are separate. A dedicated role per field (SET ROLE per request) would close it, at the cost of one role and grant set per field. | Recorded so the risk is known before hosted mode goes live (P4-6). | open |

## Phase 4, plan phase 22 (deployment)

Choices made while building the deployment: Caddy, `server/setup.sh`, `server/backup.sh`, `server/restore.sh`, their test and `server/docs/DEPLOY.md` (ADR-P4-10 as amended; 2026-10-20). Tested in Docker on one computer with Caddy's own CA, not yet on a real server with a real domain and certificate. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| S22-1 | One Compose file for the trial and the deployment, or two? ADR-P4-10 publishes the server on `ATALAIA_PORT` and adds Caddy as an option. | One `server/docker-compose.yml` (project name `atalaia`) with Caddy, the server and PostgreSQL; only Caddy publishes ports (`ATALAIA_BIND`, `ATALAIA_HTTP_PORT` 80, `ATALAIA_HTTPS_PORT` 443 TCP and UDP). `python3 tools/docker_server.py up` now runs the same file behind Caddy on `https://localhost:8443` with Caddy's own CA (`ATALAIA_TLS=internal`) and never reads an operator's `.env`; `ATALAIA_PORT` is gone. Values come from `server/.env`. A `backup-crypt` service (profile `tools`, never started by `up`) is the backups' encryption. | What is tried locally and tested is what is deployed. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-2 | Which Caddy, and set up how? | `caddy:2.11.7-alpine` pinned by digest (released 2026-10-03; it fixes 2.11.6's streams cut after a minute, which would hit the controllers' WebSocket). `server/Caddyfile`: the site `ATALAIA_DOMAIN`, ACME by default with no account email (Let's Encrypt no longer mails expiry notices; Caddy renews by itself), `ATALAIA_TLS=internal` picks Caddy's own CA through a snippet (any other value is a start-up error); the admin endpoint off; `Permissions-Policy`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`, `X-Permitted-Cross-Domain-Policies` added, `Server`, `Via` and `X-Powered-By` removed (the app already sends HSTS, `nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options`, the CSP and the referrer policy, so Caddy does not repeat them); `stream_close_delay 5m` so a reload does not cut the controllers' sockets at once; no body size limit (the app has its own). | ADR-P4-10 recommends Caddy; the security headers stay in one place each. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-3 | How are backups protected? (ADR-P4-10's script writes plain `pg_dump` files.) | Encrypted, and the dump never touches the disk in clear: `pg_dump -Fc` in the PostgreSQL container piped through `python -m atalaia_server.backup_crypt seal` in the server's image with no network (`network_mode: none`, read-only), AES-256-GCM in the STREAM construction (64 KiB chunks, nonce = random 7-byte prefix + counter + last flag, the header as AAD; `cryptography`'s AESGCM, tested with McGrew and Viega's GCM test cases and every kind of damage), under `ATALAIA_BACKUP_KEY` (32 random bytes in `.env`). Each new backup is opened in full before it is named; files 0600 in a 0700 folder; 30 days kept (`ATALAIA_BACKUP_DAYS`), the new one never deleted; one at a time (a lock, taken over from a killed backup). The key must be kept away from the server: lost key, unreadable backups (DEPLOY.md says so plainly). | Backups hold personal data and are meant to be copied off the machine. `gpg` or `age` are on neither the host nor the images; `openssl enc` has no authentication; the server's image already pins `cryptography`. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-4 | How does the server see a client's address behind Caddy (the rate limits and the audit log need it)? | uvicorn's `--proxy-headers` (now explicit in the image's command) with `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS="*"` in the Compose file; the server's port is only on the internal `app` network, where Caddy is the only other container, and Caddy replaces any `X-Forwarded-For` a client sends. The deployment test checks that a failed login's audit entry has the client's address, not Caddy's. | Caddy's address on the Compose network is not fixed; pinning a subnet risks clashing with the host's networks. Trusting every peer is safe only because no other peer can reach the port, which the network layout guarantees. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-5 | Should the scripts refuse to run as root? | No: `setup.sh`, `backup.sh` and `restore.sh` run as any user who may use Docker and do not refuse root; DEPLOY.md recommends an ordinary user in the `docker` group and says that group is root-equivalent. Secrets are written with `umask 077`, `.env` is put back to 0600 on every run. | Whoever can run `docker` is root on the machine anyway, so refusing root protects nothing; VPS images often only have root. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-6 | Which networks? | Three: `edge` (Caddy; its published ports), `app` (Caddy and the server) and `db` (the server and PostgreSQL), `app` and `db` internal: the database and the server's port cannot be reached from outside (checked by the test from a container on `edge`) and the server cannot open a connection to the internet. The day a platform adapter (plan phase 17) needs to call out, `app` loses `internal` or the server gets an egress network, by a decision. | Least privilege: the site server makes no outbound call today (plan phase 17 ships no real adapter). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-7 | What does a restore do, and what about erasures made after the backup? | `restore.sh FILE --yes`: opens the whole backup first (damage or the wrong key: nothing changed), stops the server, drops and recreates the database, restores in one transaction that stops at the first error (`--no-owner`), starts the server (migrations bring an older backup up to date) and waits for its health. A restore brings back accounts erased after the backup; DEPLOY.md tells the operator to repeat the erasures since the backup's date (`gdpr_erasure`, `chrono_player_erasure` in the audit log). | A restore into a fresh database is what the plan asks for and what the test checks; replaying erasures automatically would need a log kept outside the database, worth its own decision. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-8 | Which settings and secrets does `setup.sh` make? ADR-P4-10's `.env` has `SECRET_KEY`, `REFRESH_SECRET`, `HOSTED_MODE`, `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`. | `ATALAIA_DOMAIN`, `ATALAIA_PUBLIC_URL` (`https://DOMAIN`), `ATALAIA_TLS=acme`, and four secrets of 32 bytes from `/dev/urandom` as hex (`od`; no Python needed on the host): `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY`, `ATALAIA_METRICS_TOKEN`, `ATALAIA_BACKUP_KEY`; `ATALAIA_BACKUP_DAYS=30`, `ATALAIA_FIRMWARE_KEYS` empty, `ATALAIA_LOG_LEVEL=info`. No `REFRESH_SECRET` (refresh tokens are random and stored hashed), `HOSTED_MODE` (plan phase 21) or `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (no CORS setting exists). Idempotent: existing values are kept, a different domain is refused. | The server's real settings (S10-1); hex is safe inside the database URL and the env file. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-9 | How is the deployment tested? | `python3 tools/docker_server.py deploy` (`tools/server_deploy.py`, CI job `server-deploy`): `setup.sh` (mode, secrets, re-run, refusals; no secret in any output), the real Compose file with `ATALAIA_TLS=internal` and the name `atalaia.test`, a client container on `edge` only (`server/tests/deploy_probe.py`) checking HTTPS against Caddy's root, the headers, the HTTP redirect, the closed database and server ports and the sync over the WebSocket through Caddy with the controller's own `ws_client.py`; one server process; `backup.sh` (encrypted, modes, pruning); `restore.sh` refusing a damaged backup and a missing `--yes`; then `down --volumes`, a fresh stack and the restore, with every table's rows compared by digest before and after and the restored API key and events used through Caddy. | The plan's "back up a database with data, restore it into a new one and run the checks against it", plus what a real deployment would break first. A real domain and certificate wait for a real server (HW1). | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |
| S22-10 | How is "one worker" (ADR-P4-2, S19-4) enforced? | The image's command says `--workers 1` (which also overrides `WEB_CONCURRENCY`); DEPLOY.md forbids `--scale server=2`; the deployment test counts the server's processes. | The rate limits and the remote GM's links live in one process's memory. | confirmed by the maintainer (2026-10-21) |

## Phase 4 follow-up: device health events (2026-10-21)

Choices made while making the device-health data of plan phase 19 real (S19-9, S19-11): the field controller and the Field Box firmware now emit `node.online`, `node.offline` and `battery.low`, which the sync already carried to the site server; and the fix for a flaky QR test in the console's browser tests. In software, tested with the C host box, the real controller and the real site server on PostgreSQL in Docker; not yet on real boards (the bench board has no battery gauge fitted). All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| FU1-1 | Who emits `node.online` and `node.offline`, and how does an event "about" a box look? | The controller in charge (never a standby, never after the game ended), in the shared `FieldController` (`simulator/atalaia_sim/field_controller.py`, so the simulator tests what runs on the Pi). `node.online` (`fw`, `tier` from the box's HELLO, when known) the first time it hears a box in a game, and when it hears it again after `node.offline`; `node.offline` (`last_seen`: the game time of its last packet) when a box it has heard is silent for the threshold. The event's node is the box's id (S19-9: "the event's node is the node it is about"), its seq the controller's own count of such events (`health_seq`, continued after a restart from the stored ones so the store's unique key never drops one), no epoch and no packet seq (it never goes on air), the schema's default priority (telemetry), `t` the game time. A box registered by a join but never heard gets neither. | The controller is the only one that knows a box went quiet; giving the event the box's node is what the site server's device health already reads. A box seq of its own avoids clashing with the box's events (the box uses seq 0 for its link-made events). | open |
| FU1-2 | Which silence threshold, and how is a flapping link kept from spamming? | `[health] node_offline_s` = 120 s by default (1-3600, 0 = off): eight missed STATEs at the boxes' default 15 s, under the site server's own 180 s "silent" and above the console's 90 s "not heard". At most one link event per box every `link_gap_ms` = min(60 s, the threshold): a change that comes sooner waits, and is logged then only if it still holds, so a link that flaps reports where it ended up, at most one event a minute per box. The threshold must stay above the boxes' `CONFIG_ATALAIA_STATE_INTERVAL_S` (up to 300 s): a field that raises that must raise this too (documented in `controller.example.toml`). | Long enough that a few lost packets are not an outage, short enough that the GM and the site learn of a dead box within a few minutes; the gap bounds what one bad box can add to the log and the sync. | open |
| FU1-3 | Where does `battery.low` come from, and how is it not logged twice? | From the box itself when it has a battery gauge (FU1-6), and from the controller as a backstop: when a box's STATE or HELLO reports a level below `[health] battery_low_pct` (20 %, the site server's `BATTERY_LOW_PCT`; 0 = off) for 30 s and the box's own `battery.low` has not arrived, the controller logs one (`percent`) about the box; once per crossing, rearmed when the level comes 5 points back above the threshold. A box's own `battery.low` marks the crossing as reported, so the controller adds none. The 30 s wait is for the box's own event, which is telemetry and may be lost. | Android props, the pod's phone and older firmware report a level without the event; boxes with the event should not get two. The site server only needs one per crossing (its flag also reads the level). | open |
| FU1-4 | What happens after a controller restart, on a takeover, and does `field.lua` see these? | After a restart (with `persist_game_key`), the seq continues, each box's link is what its last stored link event says (a box that was online has the threshold from the restart to be heard, then gets `node.offline`; one that was offline gets `node.online` at once when heard) and a `battery.low` already logged is not repeated. A standby that takes over has no such history and logs `node.online` for the boxes it hears (a duplicate of the old controller's, harmless for device health, which reads the last link event). A game's `field.lua` gets a box's own events (its `battery.low` included) but not the controller's health events. | The log should not repeat itself after a restart; scripts were written for box events, and a script that wants link state can be given it later on purpose. | open |
| FU1-5 | What does the GM console show? | The *Boxes* list (already showing battery and "not heard" after 90 s) also flags a box the controller logged `node.offline` for, and marks the battery "low" ("fraca") under 20 % or with `battery.low` logged. `GET /api/v1/nodes` and the stream's node messages carry `link` (`online`, `offline`, null) and `battery_low`; a node message also carries `seen_ms_ago`, so a `node.offline` message does not reset the box's "last heard" in the page; MQTT's `nodes/{node}/health` carries both and the real last-heard time.
