| AD-13 | Where does adoption live in the console, and who sees it? | A tab of its own, *Adotar / Adopt*, after *Join*: the field's fingerprint, *USB cable* first, then *Field card (NFC)* with its warning and what to do when one is lost, a note on the coming pairing code, the recent records. GM token only for everything (`/api/v1/adopt*`, `no-store`); a read token sees only "only the organisers can adopt boxes". Records: the last 100 in the store (`adoption.log`), method, box, MAC, port, result, whether a PIN was given; never the key or the PIN; each also a `WARNING` log line (`atalaia.adoption`). | Adoption is set-up work, not game work: the *Join* tab is for games. The card's bytes are the field's master secret: GM only and never cached, like a join payload's game key. | open |
| AD-14 | What does method 1 need from the radio invite work? | Message ids (and CBOR keys) for `PAIR_OFFER`, `PAIR_START`, `PAIR_REPLY`, `PAIR_KEY`, `PAIR_DONE`; the framing of a packet outside a game (no game key, never relayed, dropped unless adoption listens); the idle boxes' channel and schedule and the duty-cycle budget for `PAIR_OFFER`; one HKDF-SHA256 helper on both sides with one table of labels; a dispatch from `field_link.c`'s idle receive to a new `adoption_pair_*()` in `adoption.c`, and a pairing service beside the controller's invite flow; ideally the invites' AES-CCM sealing for `PAIR_KEY` (80 bytes of plaintext). The RFC 0001 amendment text is in adoption.md section 7, to be numbered after the invites' amendment. | Listed so the two pieces of work meet without either editing the other's files now. | open |

## Re-inviting boxes and the start guard (2026-10-21)

Found on the bench with real boards (a Heltec V4.3 Field Box joined a controller game over LoRa through a second Heltec running the modem firmware): a GM started a new game with no box joined (the box still showed the last game's end), and every box needed a fresh join (card, QR or serial line) for every game. RJ-1 and RJ-2 are the maintainer's decisions; the rest were chosen while building them (RFC 0001 Amendment 7, proposed; [protocol/field-frames.md](../../protocol/field-frames.md)).

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
|---|----------|---------------|-----|--------|
| RJ-1 | May a game start with no box joined? | No: Start is refused while a role the game needs has no joined node (a box or a linked Android prop), with the roles named (`409`, `start_check` in the answer and on the game). The GM can start anyway after a confirmation in the console ("Start anyway?", which sends `{"force": true}`); a forced start is logged (`game N started anyway (forced) without nodes for: ...`) and told to every console (`system` `start.forced`, an alert). A mode's minimums are warnings, never a block. `force` does not skip the firmware-update guard. | The bench found a game started with nobody in it. | maintainer (2026-10-21): accepted — refuse Start while a needed role has no joined node; "Start anyway" after a confirmation; minimums only warn |
| RJ-2 | How does a box get into the next game without a card? | The controller invites it over LoRa: a dialog on creating (or before starting) a game lists the free boxes, the previous game's ticked; each picked box gets its join payload (made as for a card, still HMAC-signed) in an INVITE sealed with AES-128-CCM under HKDF-SHA256(join key, "atalaia field frame v1"). Only an idle box takes it (no game, or its game over). Whoever has the field join key and records the radio can read later games' keys: the join key is a field secret (protocol/field-frames.md, join.md, SECURITY.md). | Joining every box again for every game was far too tedious. | maintainer (2026-10-21): accepted — invites over LoRa sealed with a key derived from the field join key; the join key becomes a field secret |
| RJ-3 | Which roles does a game need before it starts? | Every role of the game's main mode and of its role packages (each has `requires:` capabilities), unless a manifest marks one `needed: false` (no built-in mode does); a modifier brings none. A scenario's game: the roles of the boxes its manifest names (`nodes`), or, with none named, as any game. A node has joined when the controller has heard it (its HELLO first; boxes and props over Wi-Fi alike) or stored it as heard before a restart; a pre-assignment alone does not count. Warnings: an integer parameter `min_<role>s` above the joined count of that role (Hold-All's and Last Stand's `min_zones`, Master and Satellites' `min_satellites`), and `last_<role>s` at or above it (Last Stand: the countdown would run from the start). | The manifests already say which capabilities a role requires; every built-in mode's roles are its reason to exist (Master and Satellites without satellites, a Medic Kit game without kits); a scenario names its boxes. | open |
| RJ-4 | Does the remote GM (site server) get "Start anyway"? | No: its `start` is refused like the console's without force (`conflict`, the roles named); the sync's command format is unchanged. | The remote GM sees the live view, not the console's confirmation; widening the shared command format is the site server's change to make. | open |
| RJ-5 | One message type or several, and which header? | One type, `FIELD` (9), with the kind in the body (ANNOUNCE 1, DISCOVER 2, INVITE 3); `game_id` 0 (game ids are 1-65534), `source` and `seq` 0, `hop_limit` 0 (never relayed); body at most 187 bytes after a 13-byte nonce, so a frame is at most 217 bytes like any packet. | One type keeps the schema's message table small and lets the modem pass every field frame up on its shape alone. | open |
| RJ-6 | What identifies a box across games? | An 8-byte device id, random, made at the first start with a join key and kept in NVS (`device_id`), shown as 16 hex digits; in HELLO as the optional key 10 and in every ANNOUNCE. Not derived from the ESP32's MAC address. | The node id changes with every game. A random id cannot be linked to the board's Wi-Fi MAC address (which it shows when it joins Wi-Fi for updates), works the same for an Android prop, and goes with an NVS erase. | open |
| RJ-7 | Nonces under a key that lives as long as the field? | Counter-based, never random (amended by RJ-15): `HMAC-SHA256(K_n, sender id)[0..7] \| boot (3) \| ctr (2)` in the clear after the header, `K_n` from the join key (HKDF, `atalaia field nonce v1`); AES-128-CCM with an 8-byte MIC, AAD the header without `hop_limit`. Sender ids: a box's device id (its boot count in NVS); a controller's fresh from `os.urandom` at every start, never stored (independent review 2026-10-21: a stored one could roll back with a restored backup or a copied database), its boot count only in that run. A box's NVS must not be cloned to another board. | A stored boot count and a frame count make one sender's nonces unique, distinct sender ids make senders' differ; no RNG quality is assumed. | open |
| RJ-8 | How is an old invite kept from pulling a box into an ended game? | Every announcement carries the box's token (8 random bytes, new at every start and every 10 minutes); both tokens are dropped whenever the box answers an invite (whatever the result), joins a game in any way (card, QR, serial, invite) and when its game ends (independent review 2026-10-21: a refused invite stayed valid for up to 20 minutes); an INVITE must carry the current or the previous one, or it is refused without an answer. The controller refuses an announcement that is not newer than the last it heard from that box (its nonce's boot and count), and forgets a game's invites when it ends. A box also refuses a join for any of the last four games it played to their end (answer "ended"). | A token works like a challenge without a clock: a recorded invite played back never matches again; the ended-games list covers a valid token with a stale game. | open |
| RJ-9 | A join payload is up to 448 bytes; the modem's payload at most 200. | Split into 1-3 INVITE parts of up to 153 bytes (the other fields take at most 34 of the 187), put back together by part number on the box; a part of another invite, token or part count starts over. Nothing is left out of the payload (a box joined by invite keeps its field CA and addresses for firmware updates). A typical box's join (115-300 bytes) is one or two parts; "too big" stays as a console status for a payload that cannot fit, which the join's own limit rules out. | Leaving out the optional fields would make boxes joined by invite unable to update their firmware; refusing would refuse common games (parameters, HTTPS). | open |
| RJ-10 | What does the USB modem do with field frames? | It carries them as they are and never holds the field key: FIELD_TX (0x09, id + frame), FIELD_RX (0x0A, frame + RSSI + SNR) and FIELD_ACK (0x0B, id + status), with or without a game configured; it checks only the shape, forces `hop_limit` 0, counts the duty cycle as for any packet, answers `busy` (new status 8) during a MEASURE; IDENT says `"field": true`. Its radio tag sets bit 31, so a FIELD_ACK never mixes with a TX_ACK. The I/O pod does not pass field frames to its phone. | The join key stays on the controller; the modem is configured per game and must carry field frames before the next game is configured, and between games. | open |
| RJ-11 | Timings and the duty cycle? | A free box: its first announcement 2-20 s after its game ends (or after it starts), then every max(60 s, time on air / (2 % x the sub-band's duty cycle)) ±10 % (about 72 s on 869.4-869.65 MHz, 12 minutes on a 1 % sub-band, 2 hours on 0.1 %), a DISCOVER answered after 0-3 s unless it announced in the last quarter of that interval, an invite answered after 100-600 ms (once per invite in 10 s, the last eight invites remembered, at most six answers a minute in all). The controller: one DISCOVER per 10 s at most, invites one at a time paced by their time on air, resent after 6 s with the box's latest token, three attempts. Numbers: an 80-byte announcement is 144 ms on air, so 7.2 s an hour (2 % of 360 s) on 869.525 MHz; a two-part invite about 0.55 s, a three-part one 1.03 s; twenty boxes with two parts take about 11 s of the controller's 360 s an hour (3 %), 30 % of a 1 % sub-band's. | A box between games should leave its budget for its next game; the GM should not wait long. | open |
| RJ-12 | What does a box show between games? | When it joins from an invite: "Invited", "game: role" (TFT and OLED). 30 s after its game ends, the final screen gives way to "Ready", "next game", unless the preset picker was opened meanwhile; new screen references `status_invited.png` and `status_ready.png` (TFT and OLED). `@lobby` on the console prints the device id, whether it is free, its channel and its invites. | The final screen left on for good was what made the bench look stuck; short words fit the OLED's head line. | open |
| RJ-13 | The console's dialog? | Opens after a game is created when free boxes have been heard (else the "Invite boxes" button, also there until Start); the previous game is the most recent other game whose HELLOs carried device ids; its boxes are ticked unless they are already in this game; the role per box defaults to its previous role when the game has it (a scenario's game: one of its box names with that role); each row shows the box's progress (sending, invited, accepted, joined, refused with the box's reason, failed). Invites live in the controller's memory only. | What the maintainer asked for; a restart in the middle of inviting only loses the progress shown (the boxes' HELLOs still count). | open |
| RJ-14 | What is left out? | Android props are not invited over the radio (they link over Wi-Fi; they count for the start guard; the app's HELLO codec reads and writes key 10). A box listens on its last game's channel: after the field's channel changes, its next game needs a card once. Field frames are not relayed: a box out of the modem's direct range is not heard between games. | Kept small; each can come later (a Wi-Fi invite for props, a channel hint in the announcement, a relay rule). | open |
| RJ-15 | Is the ESP32-S3's `esp_fill_random` good enough for the nonces, the tokens and the device id? (coordinator, 2026-10-21: must be fixed) | No, so (1) **nonces no longer use any RNG**: RJ-7's layout, the box's boot count in NVS (`ff_boot`), raised and stored before the first frame of a run and when the frame count would pass 65535 (only "none stored yet" starts at 0; any other read error or a failed write stops its field frames); the controller's sender id fresh at every start, never stored; (2) **tokens and device ids** come from a seeded generator: `app_main` first collects 32 bytes with `bootloader_random_enable()` / `esp_fill_random` / `bootloader_random_disable()`, before any ADC or I2S driver starts, and each request is SHA-256 of that seed, the board's MAC, a counter and fresh `esp_fill_random` bytes. Shared vectors gained the nonce rule (`nonces` in `field_frame.json`), checked by C and Python; host tests check a box's nonces across a reboot, the wrap and a store that fails. | ESP-IDF calls the RNG truly random only while Wi-Fi or Bluetooth runs or between those calls; a nonce repeated under the field key breaks CCM. Still to check on the bench: the boot log's entropy step before the audio and battery drivers. | open |

## Pairing a box by its code (2026-10-21)

Choices made while building the third adoption method (AD-1), the pairing code over the radio, on the re-invite work's field frames (RJ-1 to RJ-15) as AD-14 asked; AD-10 (CPace) and AD-11 (the code's limits) are kept and made concrete here. RFC 0001 Amendment 8 (proposed); [protocol/adoption.md](../../protocol/adoption.md) section 7. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AD-15 | How do the pairing messages travel? | **Message type 10, `PAIR`**, with field frames' header rules (game id 0, source 0, seq 0, `hop_limit` 0, never relayed) but **in the clear** (`version_flags` 0x28): header and a CBOR body of 1-200 bytes, five kinds (OFFER, START, REPLY, KEY, DONE). Not kinds 4-7 of type 9, which are sealed under the field key the box does not have. The USB modem carries type 10 in FIELD_TX / FIELD_RX with a shape check of its own (version 1, not encrypted, game 0, 10-209 bytes) and says `"pair": true` in IDENT; the controller tells the two types apart before anything else. | What the re-invite work's author suggested; the modem, the controller's receive path and a box's both stay one path for "frames outside a game". In the clear is honest: their security is CPace's, not a layer that would need a key. | open |
| AD-16 | Which boxes show a code, and when? | An **unadopted** box with a radio, at start (once the picker is up, over it: `*` gives the picker back, `#` a new code) and on `@pair`; not during the setup portal, not while a game is joined or played. **Not an adopted box**: it moves to another field by card or cable with its admin PIN (AD-2); "adopt to another field" by code from the admin menu is left for later. A box without a screen still logs its code on the serial console. | Showing the code is the box saying "anyone who can see me may adopt me": right for a new box in the GM's hands, wrong for a field's box on the field. | open |
| AD-17 | Who encrypts what, and with which nonces? | **Only the controller encrypts**: KEY's join key and field name, AES-128-CCM (8-byte MIC, AAD `"atalaia pair key v1"` \| pair id \| sid) under a key from the run's ISK, with field-frames.md's nonce rule: `HMAC(nonce key from the ISK, sender id)[0..7] \| boot 1 \| count 0`, the sender id drawn with `os.urandom` for every run and never stored. The box only computes HMAC tags (REPLY's and DONE's), so it needs no boot count in NVS for pairing. | The coordinator's hardening rule (RJ-15): no nonce from an RNG. The key is new to every run anyway (fresh scalars on both sides), so even a repeated nonce could not meet the same key; the rule is kept so nothing depends on that. | open |
| AD-18 | Which channel does an unadopted box listen on, and how does the controller reach it? | The box: `CONFIG_ATALAIA_RADIO_DEFAULT_CHANNEL_HZ`, **869.525 MHz** by default (the radio's channel until a game sets one), logged at start. The controller: *Look for boxes on the radio* keeps the modem on 869.525 MHz for five minutes: when the field's channel is another and no game is live, it tunes the modem there and back afterwards (or as soon as a game is live); with a game live on another channel it refuses (409) until the game ends. | A box that never joined a game knows no other channel. Retuning during a live game would deafen the controller to that game's boxes. **For review:** a field that never uses 869.525 MHz still pairs there (it is the 10 % sub-band, the cheapest for the offers). | open |
| AD-19 | What bounds the box? | The code shows 5 minutes; offers every max(15 s, time on air / (5 % x the sub-band's duty cycle)) ± 10 %, only while the code shows and not during a run or a wait (about 20 frames, 0.5 % of the hour's budget on 869.525 MHz); one run per START's sid, the last eight sids never run again; REPLY at most twice per sid, DONE at most twice again for a repeated KEY within 10 s; a run waits 10 s for KEY; every failed run (a low-order point, or no KEY with the controller's confirmation in time) burns the code; a KEY that does not authenticate is ignored, not a failure (AD-25); three in a row: a minute without runs or offers, said on the screen; ten: no pairing until a restart, said on the screen until a key; a run's scalar, ISK and keys wiped as soon as unused and on every outcome. | AD-11's limits, made exact: one guess per run (10^-6), at most three a minute and ten per start, and an attacker without the code can make the box send at most two answers per run. | open |
| AD-20 | What does the controller keep and allow? | One run at a time, GM token only (`GET /api/v1/adopt/pair`, `POST .../pair/listen`, `POST .../pair {pair_id, code}`); START and KEY each sent at most three times, 3 s apart, a step failing after 10 s (`no_answer`, `no_done`); the key is sent only after the box's confirmation matches (`wrong_code` otherwise: nothing more goes); DONE accepted only with its tag and, when the box says adopted, this field's fingerprint (`fingerprint` otherwise); offers forgotten a minute after the last; frames of runs that are over ignored. Every outcome logged and recorded with the other adoptions (method `radio`, the box's name and pair id; never the code, the key or the run's secrets). The console polls once a second while its pairing card is open (5 minutes at most). | The join key, the field's master secret, leaves the controller only for a box that proved it shows the code the GM typed. | open |
| AD-21 | The exchange's parameters? | CPace `CPACE-X25519-SHA512`, initiator the controller: `PRS` the 6 ASCII digits, `CI` = `lv_cat("atalaia pair v1", pair id)`, `sid` 16 bytes from the controller, `ADa` = "atalaia controller", `ADb` = "atalaia box"; from the ISK, HKDF-SHA256 with the sid as salt and the labels `atalaia pair confirm a v1`, `... confirm b v1`, `... seal v1`, `... nonce v1`; 16-byte tags, HMAC-SHA256 over fixed labels (DONE's also over its result and fingerprint). Monocypher's Elligator 2 gets `min(r, p - r)` (AD-10). | Explicit key confirmation both ways before anything secret moves; the pair id in CI binds the run to the box that offered it. | open |
| AD-22 | What does a box say about itself on the air before it is adopted? | A **pair id** (8 random bytes, new at every pairing: neither its MAC nor its later device id), its name and tier, in the clear. | Enough for the GM to pick it in the console; nothing that follows the box afterwards. The name is whatever the box was given (`node_name`), visible to anyone listening while the code shows. | open |
| AD-23 | How are the two sides checked? | `protocol/vectors/pairing.json` (`tools/pairing_vectors.py`, `--check` in check.sh and CI): the CPace draft's B.1 and B.1.10 **copied from the draft** (the tool refuses to write if `atalaia_sim.cpace` disagrees), a whole run with fixed scalars, sid and pair id (keys, tags, the sealed key, the five frames, a wrong code's answer), six generators found by a fixed search, four of them hashing to an r of 2^254 or more (the branch where Monocypher gets p - r; without it the box's generator differs, checked), and 19 bodies and 6 frames to refuse. C: `core/tests/test_pair_frame.c` (with mbedtls's AES-CCM: the sealed bytes) and `firmware/esp32/test/test_box_pair.c` (Monocypher against the draft; the run played against the box, its REPLY and DONE compared byte for byte; KEY re-sealed with the core's test stub because the firmware's host tests, like CI's firmware job, have no mbedtls). Python: `simulator/tests/test_pair_frame.py`. End to end: the C host box through the C host modem (`controller/tests/test_pairing.py`, a browser test). | Known answers from the source document for the primitive; this project's own cases for its framing, cross-checked by two implementations. | open |
| AD-24 | What is left? | On the boards (BRING_UP step 10a.5): the offers heard by the modem, a wrong code then the right one, range, the OLED's code legible; the bench log's entropy step (RJ-15) before the first code is drawn; "adopt to another field" by code on an adopted box (AD-16); the app's Android props are not paired this way (they link over Wi-Fi). | Outside the software. | open |
| AD-25 | Can a radio attacker without the code end a real run, or silently lock a box? (independent review 2026-10-21, finding 1, medium) | No longer the first; the second is now said. A **KEY whose confirmation does not match, or whose sealed key does not open, is ignored and counted**, and the run keeps waiting for the controller's own KEY until its 10 s run out; before, any KEY with the sniffed sid ended the run. A START while a run is on is still refused, not a replacement; the console's "no answer" now says that another pairing may be under way with the box (interference) and to try again. After three failed runs the screen says **PAIR PAUSED, for 1 minute** (no code shown); after ten **PAIR LOCKED, restart the box** until a key, instead of going back to the picker with only a log line. A START that got a REPLY still burns the code (its tag is one guess). adoption.md's threat model now says that a radio attacker can always deny pairing (jamming, taking the run, the pauses and the lock) and never adopt the box. | The run slot and the code's limits are what bound the guesses, so they stay; what an unauthenticated frame may no longer do is end a run it could not complete. Tests: test_box_pair.c (a forged KEY and a tampered sealed key mid-run, then the real KEY adopts; the wrong code fails only at the timeout; the paused and locked screens). | open |
| AD-26 | Can a fake box get extra guesses by offering under a real box's name? (independent review, finding 2, low) | Made visible: the box's pairing screen shows **Id: the pair id's first two bytes**, and the console shows each offer's short id, RSSI and how long ago it was heard; two offers under one name are flagged, and an OFFER reusing a known pair id under another name is ignored (the first name stays) and flags the entry; after a wrong code the console says to read the box's screen again (a real box shows a new code after any failed run; if it did not change, that entry was not the box). | A fake entry can still take the GM's guesses, one per run, but never the key, and the GM now has what it takes to tell the entries apart. Tests: test_pairing.py (duplicate names, a reused pair id), the browser test (the id beside the entry is the box's). | open |
| AD-27 | Can the console say "listening on 869.525 MHz" while the modem is elsewhere? (independent review, finding 3, low) | No: whenever the engine tunes the modem to the field's channel (the channel set, a scan starting or ending, the modem reconnecting), pairing clears its listening window, and a run under way fails as `channel_changed` unless the field's channel is 869.525 MHz itself. | The modem has one channel; the console must not claim what the radio is not doing. Tests: test_pairing.py (each path). | open |

## The re-invites on the bench boards (2026-10-22)

Choices made while running the re-invites (RJ) and the start guard on the two Heltec V4.3 bench boards, a controller in Docker and board 2 as its modem. Before these fixes, an invite never completed on real radios, while every software test had passed. All open.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BN-1 | Why did invites time out on the boards? | Each `recv()` call set packet params, cleared IRQs and started a single 50 ms RX, then waited 100 ms for RX_DONE; a packet longer than that (an invite part of 217 bytes is ~330 ms at SF7) was abandoned and the next call restarted RX over it. Now the SX1262 stays in continuous RX (`sx_start_rx()` once, re-armed only after TX, CAD, standby/sleep or a channel change, tracked by `rx_armed`), and `recv()` waits up to 50 ms for a finished packet, clears the IRQ at once and leaves the chip listening. CRC and header errors are dropped (they were passed up before; the MIC still protected the content). `send()` goes through standby and drops a stale DIO1 edge before SetTx, so an RX_DONE cannot pass for TX_DONE. | Found on the bench: the box logged one 217-byte part in six, and none of the second parts. The same deaf gap explains the earlier lost STATE packets and a box left in STANDBY after Start (a START right before its TIME beacon). Applies to the box, the modem and the pod (one HAL). | open |
| BN-2 | Why did the second part never arrive even then? | The controller queued all of an invite's parts in one tick and the modem sent them back to back. The inviter now sends one part per tick, the next one `PART_GAP_MS` (150 ms) after the previous part's time on air; an invite counts as "invited" when its first part is out. | A receiver needs time after each packet before it hears the next preamble (~12 ms at SF7); with BN-1 the gap is smaller, but pacing costs nothing at this rate. | open |
| BN-3 | How fast does a box see Start now? | Not measured yet: the next bench game times it. Expected well under a second when the START is heard (its TIME beacon follows at once). | Before BN-1 the START was often lost and the box waited for a retry or the next beacon (the maintainer pressed the button while it still showed STANDBY). | open |
| BN-4 | Why did the box crash-loop after a reflash mid-game? | `box_game_resume()` kept the stored join's buffer (448 B), the decoded join (~750 B) and the saved state (~1.1 KB, with the mode's 1 KB snapshot) on the main task's 8 KB stack, then ran the mode's restore: a stack overflow on every boot with a saved running game. They are now static (resume runs once, at boot, on one task). | The first boot with a saved running game on the boards since the lobby and adoption were added. | open |
| BN-5 | A box that reboots more than 16 times in one game can no longer be heard: its epoch is past the receivers' window (RFC 0001: try the current epoch and the next; the modem tries 16). What should resync it? | **For the maintainer**; nothing changed. Seen after BN-4's crash loop (35 reboots: epoch 91 to 126): the modem counted 57 authentication failures and the game lost the box. Options: an unauthenticated-epoch hint in the header for HELLO (with a bounded search), a field-frame resync (the box asks, sealed under the field key, and the controller answers with the epoch it will accept), or accept that a new game is needed after a crash loop. | Rare (a crash loop), but the box then silently drops out of the game. | open |
| BN-6 | What does a free box show after a restart? | **Follow-up**: today the box shows its admin-PIN lock (the picker) until its first game ends, and only then "Ready / next game". An adopted, free box should show "Ready" from boot too, so a GM does not take the PIN screen for a box that cannot be invited (the maintainer was misled by it on the bench). | UX found on the bench. | open |

## The invite dialog says when the game is ready, and starts it (2026-10-22)

The maintainer asked on the bench for the GM console's *Boxes for this game* dialog (`controller/console/src/components/box-invites.js`) to say when the boxes joined so far are enough, and to start the game from it. In the console only; tested in Chromium against the real controller.

| # | Question | Answer chosen | Why | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GM-0 | The dialog should say clearly when every role has a box (counts per role, unmet minimums as warnings) and offer "Start game", asking "Review the game's other settings first?" (Review settings / Start now; Start anyway through the same force path when a role has no box). | Built as GM-1 to GM-4. | The GM could not tell when the dialog could be closed. | maintainer (2026-10-22): requested |
| GM-1 | Where does the dialog's "ready" come from? Does it need a new endpoint (`GET /api/v1/games/{id}/readiness`)? | No new endpoint: the live, ready game the controller pushes already carries the engine's `start_check` (`needed`, `joined` per role, `missing`, `warnings`), and the engine pushes the game again on every HELLO (and `GET .../start-check` gives the same). The dialog only words it: *Ready to start: every role has a box (1 × zone)* in a live region, or *Still waiting for a box for: …* with the counts, and each unmet minimum as a warning. A controller test pins that the pushed check carries the counts and the warnings. | One implementation of the rule, the engine's; the console computes nothing. | open |
| GM-2 | What does the dialog's Start do? | *Start game* (or *Start anyway*, red, while a role has no box) opens a confirmation in the dialog: *Review the game's other settings first?* with *Review settings*, *Start now* (or *Start anyway*, naming the missing roles) and *Back* (Escape too). It calls the same `POST .../start` as the game card's Start, with `{"force": true}` only when a role is missing, so a forced start is logged and told to every console (`start.forced`) as before; if the controller refuses (a box left meanwhile, a firmware update on its way) the dialog stays open and the error shows. The dialog appears only for the GM token, on the field's leader, for a ready game; the remote GM is unchanged and cannot force (RJ-4). Undeclared chronograph checks show as a warning in the confirmation. | The maintainer's wording; the existing guard's rules and path, not a second one. | open |
| GM-3 | What is the "game's settings" view that *Review settings* shows? | A new *Game settings* section on the game card (read-only): the game's parameters as created, labelled in the console's language, next to the duration, teams and squads the card already shows. *Review settings* closes the dialog, opens that section, scrolls to the card and focuses it; the game stays ready. Settings cannot be changed once a game is created (end it and create another; the section says so); teams' names and crests still can, below. | There was no view of a created game's parameters; editing them after creation is a bigger change nobody asked for. | open |
| GM-4 | Accessibility | Readiness in `role="status"` `aria-live="polite"`; the confirmation is a labelled group, focus moves to its first choice (*Review settings*, the safe one) and back to Start when it closes; Escape closes the confirmation, then the dialog. | Keyboard and screen-reader use, as the console's other dialogs. | open |
